" alt= " University of Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;27937;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1245893 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Children who are not getting enough sleep and spending a significant amount of time looking at screens have a higher likelihood of making poor decisions , a study published on Wednesday found . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Michelle Guerrero , lead author and postdoctoral fellow at the CHEO Research Institute and the University of Ottawa said her study looked at the correlation between screen time and behaviour in adolescents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We were looking at how excessive screen time leads to impulsive behaviour , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guerrero&amp;rsquo ; s study defined impulsive behaviour as acting &amp;ldquo ; without realizing the risk involved or the consequences involved with the behaviour . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Additionally , impulsive behaviour is often connected with mental health issues and is a key component of ADHD. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When we don&amp;rsquo ; t think before we act&amp;hellip ; it has huge impacts on our lives , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guerrero said a key discovery in the study was how easily preventable this behaviour is as it involves a minor life adjustment including less screen time and more sleep . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The behaviours we looked at in our study are modifiable , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; They can be changed right now , today . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said her study found children who have their screen time limited to under two hours a day and sleep between nine to eleven hours a night &amp;ldquo ; are less impulsive . &amp;rdquo ; Exercise was also found to be a factor that led to less impulsive children . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There has been a significant amount of research on how screens can affect children&amp;rsquo ; s mental health , Guerrero said , and found the connection to impulsive behaviour unsurprising . Additionally , she said the benefit of a full-night&amp;rsquo ; s sleep is a major determinant of one&amp;rsquo ; s behaviour . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When people don&amp;rsquo ; t sleep long enough&amp;hellip ; Their behaviour is more impulsive , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The paper analysed data for 4,524 children and was titled 24-Hour Movement Behaviours and Impulsivity . It is part of the Adolescent Brain Cognitive Development Study , which intends to follow participants for 10 years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The paper was published in Pediatrics and was authored by Guerrero alongside members of the Healthy Active Living and Obesity Research Group at the CHEO Research Institute . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27967;TOOLONG ... Kitts to step down as CEO and president of The Ottawa Hospital&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28002;TOOLONG ... to view the Perseid Meteor shower this week&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28037;TOOLONG ... breached conflict of interest law in SNC-Lavalin affair , ethics commissioner rules&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Luke CarrollThu , 15 Aug 2019 14:57:22 **45;28072;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenPersonal TechWomen'They know they are not alone ' : In Renfrew , community paramedics make house calls and change liveshttp : **35;28119;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28156;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie , a community care paramedic , looks after Ed Pinnell at his home in Arnrprior July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie , a community care paramedic , looks after Ed Pinnell at his home in Arnrprior July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;28192;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432716 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;28222;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Maria Makkos greets Stephanie Rose at the front door of Makkos&amp;rsquo ; s Arnprior apartment building . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You are here to see me ? &amp;rdquo ; she asks , grinning broadly as she leads the community paramedic to her tidy unit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over her shoulder , Rose , the paramedic , carries a heavy black bag containing a blood pressure cuff , equipment for drawing blood , papers and other medical equipment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Makkos , 82 , is the third client the community paramedic has seen this morning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rose checks Makkos&amp;rsquo ; s blood pressure , which is high . When they sort through her medication , Rose discovers the elderly woman hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been taking it regularly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You are busted , I caught you red-handed , &amp;rdquo ; says Rose with a laugh and a wag of the finger . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28258;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;28294;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432715 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Makkos , who still drives and walks to stay fit , says she is determined to remain healthy so she doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have to go into the hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Don&amp;rsquo ; t&amp;rsquo ; worry , that is the last place we want you to be , &amp;rdquo ; says Rose . &amp;ldquo ; We are going to help you stay here , but we just want to make sure you are safe . &amp;rdquo ; Taking her medicine on time , she tells Makkos , is crucial to staying healthy and in her own home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before she leaves , Makkos wistfully jokes that she wants Rose&amp;rsquo ; s visit to last all day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a role reminiscent of a family doctor doing house calls , Rose visits clients to check on their health and just to chat . The conversations , sprinkled with laughter , are as important as the testing to determine what people need to remain independent and as healthy as possible , she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is important to build a relationship first in order to understand what kind of support clients need , says Rose . Visiting patients in their homes makes that easier . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A lot of time people get to doctor&amp;rsquo ; s appointments and get this white coat syndrome , they forget what is bothering them . When I get there on scene in their home , they are a little more relaxed and they actually talk about what is bothering them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rose takes that information back to their family doctor . She is working under a provincial program called Health Links in which paramedics make routine home visits to high-needs patients , in close communications with their family doctor and other health professionals . Electronic health records allow test results and notes from paramedics to be uploaded to doctors offices . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28324;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;28360;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432713 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renfrew County paramedics like Rose visit about 150 patients as part of the Health Links program , which is aimed at providing better coordinated care to some of the highest needs patients in the region . An additional 1,500 patients , who are not part of Health Links but also have high needs or difficulty getting to health appointments , also receive regular visits from Renfrew County community paramedics. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Community paramedics have become a key link in the chain that is the health system in Renfrew . They are helping to keep people out of hospital , reduce 911 calls and improve clients&amp;rsquo ; quality of life &amp;mdash ; and their families . Their work supports family physicians , community nurses and other health professionals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You become a family , &amp;rdquo ; says Rosemary Drziak . Her husband Ed Pinnell , an aerospace engineer with the National Research Council , is now being treated for malignant brain cancer . He is Rose&amp;rsquo ; s first client of the day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The couple&amp;rsquo ; s Arnprior home has recently been fitted with a ramp for Pinnell , who uses a wheelchair since falling and breaking his hip . Seizures and complications from brain cancer put him at high risk of falling , something Drziak worries about even if she takes her eyes off him to take a shower . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rose sits close to Pinnell&amp;rsquo ; s wheelchair and asks him about seizures . He hasn&amp;rsquo ; t had any since she last saw him . &amp;ldquo ; That is one thing you were worried about , that they were becoming more frequent , &amp;rdquo ; she says to Pinnell , as she prepares to draw blood . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She asks about headaches , checks his blood pressure and blood sugar levels and talks to Pinnell and Drziak before heading off . Sweets are OK , she tells Pinnell with a smile , noting his blood sugar levels are not too high . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Community paramedics make regular home visits , but can also be reached 24 hours a day if there is a need . The program offers a crucial safety net for the couple as they deal with Pinnell&amp;rsquo ; s health challenges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We can not say enough , &amp;rdquo ; says Drziak. &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t know what we would do without them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rose says community paramedics become like family members to some of their clients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They know they are not alone . We are that family that can speak for them , on their behalf . Their advocate . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;28390;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28426;TOOLONG ... future of health care has arrived -- but is the Ontario government paying attention ? &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28461;TOOLONG ... cottages , tall trees and a barn : Canada builds first-ever village in B.C to house residents with dementia&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28496;TOOLONG ... ticks are so common in parts of Canada , testing no longer done&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneThu , 25 Jul 2019 12:03:02 **45;28531;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenThe future of health care has arrived -- but is the Ontario government paying attention ? http : **30;28578;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28610;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;28646;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432715 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;28676;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Is this the future of health care ? And is the Ontario government paying attention ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The drone hovered before carefully dropping its payload &amp;mdash ; a small defibrillator &amp;mdash ; on target . Nearby , three paramedic executives from Germany watched the demonstration closely . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drones , which have delivered medicine to flood victims and given rescuers a birds-eye view of stranded homeowners in recent months , are just part of what drew the Germans to Renfrew County to see local paramedics at work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They recently spent a week training with paramedics and taking notes . In addition to learning about the extensive use of drones , the Germans followed community paramedics doing their rounds visiting chronically ill patients in their homes . They also learned about technological innovations such as a specially designed electronic health records system for community para-medicine. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Germans are not the first to make the trek to the Ottawa Valley to learn from local paramedics . Renfrew County paramedics have helped rewrite the book on what it means to be a paramedic in a largely rural community . They have addressed challenges and changed local health care in the process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The world is paying attention . But is the Ontario government ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28712;TOOLONG ... title= " Brian Leahey ( centre ) , Deputy Chief of the Renfrew Paramedic Service , teaches Paramedic Sebastian Gerosch ( left ) and Paramedic Sebastian Lange about their drone program in Renfrew Thursday July 4 , 2019 . The two paramedics are here from Germany to learn what Renfrew County is doing to improve their service . " alt= " Brian Leahey ( centre ) , Deputy Chief of the Renfrew Paramedic Service , teaches Paramedic Sebastian Gerosch ( left ) and Paramedic Sebastian Lange about their drone program in Renfrew Thursday July 4 , 2019 . The two paramedics are here from Germany to learn what Renfrew County is doing to improve their service . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;28748;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432712 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renfrew paramedic officials and politicians are concerned that changes announced by Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s Progressive Conservative government earlier this year could mean the loss of what makes the local paramedic service special . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Not only are its programs designed to meet specific needs of Renfrew residents and tackle difficult health and geographical challenges , but they are considered among the most innovative in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Renfrew County , about an **25;28778;TOOLONG ; s drive northwest of Ottawa , the paramedic service functions as a kind of wire that connects and energizes all the components of the local health system . Their work in the community reduces hospital visits and stays by some of the highest-needs residents ; supports family medicine teams to help them function with large numbers of patients , as is common in rural areas ; and has technology to help remote residents remain in their homes and to deal with emergency situations . All that , in addition to the more traditional roles of responding to 911 calls , stabilizing sick patients and getting them to hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , the future for Renfrew paramedics , and paramedics around the province , is unclear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28805;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Maria Makkos , 82 , during their initial consultation at Maria 's home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;28841;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432715 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ontario government has announced it will amalgamate the 52 municipally run paramedic services across Ontario to create 10 regional services . Along with amalgamation will come upgraded communications systems , says Health Minister Christine Elliott , who promises it will result in a more efficient and effective system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " By better integrating dispatch and paramedic services , the ministry will improve coordination and ensure that resources are being used for frontline patient care in a model that is sustainable for the years to come , " said health ministry spokesman Mark Nesbitt in a statement . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renfrew paramedic officials say they want to make sure the government knows the kind of work being done across the county and in other parts of the province before it makes massive changes . &lt;/p&gt; **28;28871;TOOLONG : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;28901;TOOLONG ... know they are not alone ' : In Renfrew , community paramedics make house calls and change **26;28936;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Chief Mike Nolan argues that the Renfrew paramedic services is achieving exactly what the province wants with its reset of the health system . Renfrew&amp;rsquo ; s innovative community paramedic program has reduced 911 calls by 24 per cent among a population of chronically ill residents who have been frequent 911 users in the past . The result is improved individual health outcomes at a lower cost to the health system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The program that we are running in Renfrew County fulfills and , in many cases , exceeds the interests of this government in reforming the health-care system , &amp;rdquo ; says Nolan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nolan says Renfrew County officials want to inform the province that there are models of care in place &amp;mdash ; in Renfrew and elsewhere &amp;mdash ; that should be expanded across the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His concern is that amalgamation will water down paramedic programs to &amp;ldquo ; the lowest common denominator&amp;rdquo ; which will mean the loss of innovation , technological advances and local relevance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nolan and others are hoping to lead changes to paramedic services across the country and see those innovations expanded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;28964;TOOLONG ... title= " Renfrew paramedic chief Mike Nolan . " alt= " Renfrew paramedic chief Mike Nolan . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;29000;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432714 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renfrew paramedics have already helped drive change in paramedic services across Ontario and around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When it comes to community paramedics , Renfrew County is known as one of the leading programs in the world , says Sebastian Lange , one of the visiting Bavarian paramedics . Renfrew has also seen experts fly in from Ireland , England , Australia , New Zealand , the U.S. , Turkey , the Netherlands and several other Canadian provinces to learn about its paramedic model . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The county is the largest , geographically , in the province &amp;mdash ; stretching from Ottawa to Algonquin Park &amp;mdash ; and , like many rural parts of the province , serves a population that is older , has lower socioeconomic status and is more likely to have chronic disease than more urban parts of the province . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Its use of technology is , in part , an answer to some of those geographic challenges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Renfrew County paramedic services pioneered the use of drone technology to assist first responders . It has worked closely with Transport Canada and is the only paramedic organization in the country that has permission to use drones beyond the line of sight , says deputy chief Brian Leahey , who is in charge of the program . Leahey is also a pilot . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Drones are used frequently by Renfrew paramedics , for a variety of tasks . They can deliver supplies , and defibrillators , to remote areas faster than paramedics can get there . In fact , paramedics are looking at keeping a permanent drone on location to deliver defibrillators to more isolated parts of the county . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Recently , drones have also been used to assess emergency situations &amp;mdash ; including in flood zones or accident scenes . The unmanned aerial system , as it is known , works closely with the service&amp;rsquo ; s remote access team , and others . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The community paramedic program tackles demographic challenges in Renfrew County by making house calls and doing remote monitoring of some of the sickest patients in the area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bill Craig is among its clients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Craig , 64 , of Arnprior , is a diabetic and kidney transplant patient who was a frequent user of 911 services until the community medicine program began monitoring him 24/7. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Community paramedics would visit him at home regularly to check his blood pressure and blood sugar , among other things . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have found it really beneficial , &amp;rdquo ; says Craig . &amp;ldquo ; When they leave here , they don&amp;rsquo ; t just leave , they follow-up with your family doctor and they research different things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Paramedics have even travelled with Craig to visit his endocrinologist in Ottawa to better understand his medical needs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Craig says he feels more secure under the watchful eye of the community paramedic program . Paramedics have discovered him , more than once , in a diabetic coma when they came for a visit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The service works with family health teams and other medical professionals , remotely monitors some patients and is available 24/7. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Community paramedics monitor some high-needs patients with electronic systems that send digital information back to the paramedics , such as blood pressure and weight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;29030;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , chats with Bill Craig , a patient at his home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , chats with Bill Craig , a patient at his home in Arnrprior , July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;29066;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432717 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Paramedics are alerted when readings are abnormal or when readings have been missed , which can trigger a visit to the patient at home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Community paramedics also operate mobile clinics around the county that offer medical services where seniors are . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s kind of like a barber shop , in that we go to where the seniors are playing euchre and set up the corner , &amp;rdquo ; says Nolan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; People bring their medications and are given help keeping track of them . The paramedics take blood pressure , offer electro cardio grams and blood tests . &amp;ldquo ; It is not uncommon that we pick up patients that have high blood pressure . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A group of 20 community paramedics see more than 1,500 clients regularly . They monitor up to 150 patients in their homes &amp;mdash ; most of them among the five per cent of patients who use 80 per cent of health services , including Craig . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They tend to be socio-economically challenged , which is an indicator of the challenges they face managing their health , &amp;rdquo ; says Nolan . &amp;ldquo ; For many of these people , to take a taxi to go to the lab to get blood work done and to go home is a choice of whether they are going to eat that day or not , or turn up the thermostat . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Community paramedics keep in close touch with their clients&amp;rsquo ; family physicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;29096;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jeff Dodge , a former Ottawa high-tech CEO who became a Renfrew paramedic as a second career , has been key to developing some of the Renfrew paramedic organization&amp;rsquo ; s high-tech expertise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Working with the Quebec-based software company Prehos , the paramedic service developed an electronic records system for community paramedics . It went live in Renfrew County last year and is now used by the majority of paramedic services in Eastern Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Using the system , community paramedics have access to client records on tablets and can share notes and data with family doctors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dodge , who makes home visits as a community paramedic , recalls writing a note to a client&amp;rsquo ; s doctor after one of his visits . &amp;ldquo ; Before I left the house , the doctor had it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;29132;TOOLONG ... title= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Ed Pinnell at his home in Arnrprior July 10 , 2019. " alt= " Stephanie Rose , a community care paramedic , looks after Ed Pinnell at his home in Arnrprior July 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;29168;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1432718 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renfrew&amp;rsquo ; s community paramedic program has reduced 911 calls and emergency room visits , improved paramedic response times , and reduced hospital admission rates and length of stays when clients had to be admitted , according to analysis . It also improved and increased communication among clients and their health-care providers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The program has saved money and improved the quality of life of clients , according to research conducted to measure the program&amp;rsquo ; s value . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nolan thinks the work being done by paramedics in Renfrew contains valuable lessons for the Ontario government , which has vowed to make changes to help relieve an overburdened health system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We think we have a unique position in the community to solve many of the problems presented as hallway medicine . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29198;TOOLONG ... hunger : ' Residents in three Ottawa ridings are among highest food bank users in the province&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29233;TOOLONG ... ER visits are on the rise -- and it 's the tip of a very large iceberg&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29268;TOOLONG ... or starting exercise in middle age tied to longer life&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneThu , 25 Jul 2019 11:34:15 **45;29303;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **27;29350;TOOLONG ER visits are on the rise -- and it 's the tip of a very large iceberghttp : **33;29379;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29414;TOOLONG ... title= " New research tracks alcohol use . " alt= " New research tracks alcohol use . " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images " **28;29450;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1347813 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;29480;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; More people are ending up in Ontario emergency rooms because of alcohol use , according to a study headed by Ottawa researchers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those ER visits are increasing faster among women and young people between the ages of 25 and 29 , according to the study published Monday in the Canadian Medical Association Journal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div class= " page " title= " Page 1 " &gt; &lt;div class= " layoutArea " &gt; &lt;div class= " column " &gt; &lt;p&gt;Researchers at the Institute for Clinical Evaluative Sciences , The Ottawa Hospital , the Bruy ? re Research Institute and the University of Ottawa sifted through 2003-2016 hospital administrative data from across the province . In that time , there were more than 765,000 emergency room visits in Ontario attributable to alcohol . The rate of alcohol-related emergency department visits increased 4.4 times more than the rate of overall visits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;The top reason for alcohol-related ER visits was intoxication , accounting for 44.6 per cent of visits , followed by harmful use at 21.4 per cent of visits , withdrawal at 11.4 per cent and alcohol dependence at 10.9 per cent . Alcohol-related ER visits peaked for women around legal drinking age . For men , it was in their early 50s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; ER visits are the &amp;ldquo ; tip of the iceberg , &amp;rdquo ; said senior author Dr. Peter Tanuseputro , a researcher , physician and assistant professor at the University of Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The bodily harms are often not the biggest issue . It&amp;rsquo ; s the impact on the family . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Men were still at more risk of alcohol-related harm , but women are catching up . Between 2003 and 2016 , alcohol-related ER visits by women ages 25-29&amp;nbsp ; increased by 240 per cent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Young people are drinking in more harmful patterns , and they&amp;rsquo ; re not aging out of it , said lead researcher Dr. Daniel Myran , a family physician and public health and preventive medicine resident at the University of Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s a generational effect . Younger women who were drinking in the early 2000s are still drinking more . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The numbers stem from the period before Ontario started to increase the ways people could access booze , which raises questions about Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s new policies around buck-a-beer , tailgating parties and making alcohol available in more locations for longer hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Increasing prices for alcoholic beverages through taxation or price minimums have been proven to be effective policies to reduce alcohol consumption , particularly for younger drinkers and heavy drinkers , said Dr. Sheryl Spithoff , a family and addiction medicine physician at Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;br&gt; Women&amp;rsquo ; s College Hospital . Government retail outlets and limiting hours of operation and outlet density are measures that have been endorsed by the World Health Organization . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Ontario government has gone in the opposite direction with such glee and without any embarrassment , &amp;rdquo ; said Timothy Stockwell , director of the Canadian Institute for Substance Use Research at the University of Victoria . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He points out that there are peaks and troughs in alcohol consumption that appear to coincide with economic factors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There was falling alcohol consumption around the time of the financial crisis . But about the time of the recovery , people started to drink again , &amp;rdquo ; Stockwell said . &amp;ldquo ; In the past five years , there has been a rapid uptick in deaths and hospital presentations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study is just one piece of evidence in a growing pile that suggests alcohol has grave costs . Among other illnesses , alcohol causes liver disease , addiction and withdrawal problems , heart damage and cancer . Cancer Care Ontario estimates that 3,000 cancer diagnoses a year could be spared in the province if people followed safe alcohol consumption guidelines . Between two and seven per cent of breast cancer cases can be attributed to alcohol consumption . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is also a massive cost to the healthcare system . Costs attributable to alcohol were $4.32 billion Canada-wide in 2014 , according to the Canadian Centre on Substance Use and Addiction and the Canadian Institute for Substance Use Research . Alcohol-related deaths increased by 26 per cent for women and by five per cent for men between 2001 and 2017 , according to the Canadian Institute for Health Information . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite the alarming statistics , there is still a lot of public ignorance about the relationship between alcohol and serious illness . According to one survey reported by Cancer Care Ontario , only one-third of Canadians are aware that they can lower their cancer risk by reducing their alcohol consumption . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prohibition is not the answer , Spithoff said . But she urges governments to ban all promotion of alcohol use , including marketing , branding and events sponsorships . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the very least , governments should consider applying the rules it uses for tobacco promotion to alcohol . Alcohol is at least as harmful as tobacco and shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be treated differently , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Tobacco kills more people . But having an alcohol addiction is more likely to result in disruption to people&amp;rsquo ; s lives . If you include harms to others , alcohol is the most harmful substance out there , &amp;rdquo ; Spithoff said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re not saying that alcohol shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be available , but that it should be available in a public health model . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spithoff points to a framework introduced in Scotland which set national prevention goals . An awareness campaign introduced this spring called &amp;ldquo ; Count 14&amp;rdquo ; was developed to demonstrate what 14 alcohol units actually means and encourages Scots to think about how their weekly drinking adds up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Canada , the guidelines for low-risk consumption are not more than two drinks a day , 10 in a week , or three drinks on a special occasion for women . For men , it&amp;rsquo ; s three drinks a day , 15 in a week , or four on a special occasion , according to the National Alcohol Strategy Advisory Committee at the Canadian Centre on Substance Use and Addiction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And a &amp;ldquo ; serving&amp;rdquo ; of alcohol is actually rather small . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A standard 750 ml bottle of wine contains more than five servings and a serving of beer is 341 ml . To compare , a pint of beer is 568 ml &amp;mdash ; so two pints a day is over a man&amp;rsquo ; s daily limit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our study shows that people aren&amp;rsquo ; t meeting those guidelines . They&amp;rsquo ; re not even close , &amp;rdquo ; Tanuseputro said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Is a glass of wine a day still considered good for health ? Not according to a &lt;a href= " https : **33;29516;TOOLONG ... published last year i&lt;/a&gt; n the British medical journal The Lancet , based on analysis of data from 195 countries and territories , which concluded that even one drink a day increases health risks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The widely held view of the health benefits of alcohol needs revising , particularly as improved methods and analyses continue to show how much alcohol use contributes to global death and disability , &amp;rdquo ; the study concluded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our results show that the safest level of drinking is none . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The emergency room study wasn&amp;rsquo ; t designed to reveal the causes behind by increases in certain groups . But Myran suggests marketing alcohol to women has become increasingly targeted and sophisticated . It&amp;rsquo ; s treated as a female empowerment issue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It can be very seductive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Reducing alcohol consumption takes a concerted effort on the part of people who are concerned about public health and safety , said Stockwell of the Institute for Substance Use Research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s pretty low down on the agenda politically . You have people in one area collecting revenue and other people who are aware , but who don&amp;rsquo ; t have the power . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stockwell is one of the researchers involved in a Health Canada-funded project introduced in the Yukon last November . Colourful warning labels were placed on alcohol bottles and cans . One label warned consumers that alcohol can cause cancer . Another outlined the recommended maximum number of drinks per day . Four weeks after the launch , the Yukon Liquor Corp . halted the research in response to concerns from the alcohol industry . The study is going ahead , but the cancer label has been dropped and replaced with one about standard drink sizes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What will it take to shift consumption levels in any meaningful way ? A calamity and some high-profile champions , said Stockwell . People think of alcohol they way they think about food and the role it plays in celebrations and bringing people together . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are all blissfully unaware that this does so much harm , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a silent problem . We don&amp;rsquo ; t question it much . It&amp;rsquo ; s like the industry owns parts of our minds . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;29551;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;29586;TOOLONG **26;29622;TOOLONG the numbers : **26;29650;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; 765,346 : Number of emergency room visits in Ontario between 2003 and 2016 that were due to alcohol , according to a study released Monday &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 32.3 per cent : Proportion of alcohol-related emergency room visitors who were female &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 17.4 per cent : Proportion of female alcohol-related emergency room visitors who were below the legal drinking age &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 9.1 per cent : Proportion of male alcohol-related emergency room visitors who were below the legal drinking age &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 77.6 per cent : Increase in the numbers of males who visited an emergency room because of alcohol between 2003 and 2016 &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusMon , 22 Jul 2019 21:45:33 **45;29783;TOOLONG &amp;amp ; FitnessLocal NewsMenWomenPellerin : Healthy Ottawa -- Ottawa , it 's time for a healthy debate about our collective wellbeinghttp : **31;29830;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29863;TOOLONG ... title= " Jake Cole of the Sierra Club Canada Foundation , left , and columnist Brigitte Pellerin enjoy the pedestrian pathways in Ottawa . " alt= " Jake Cole of the Sierra Club Canada Foundation , left , and columnist Brigitte Pellerin enjoy the pedestrian pathways in Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;29899;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425114 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;29929;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Could Ottawa become Canada 's -- or even the world 's -- healthiest city ? In an occasional summer series starting today , Brigitte Pellerin explores what it would take . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ask the people around you what a healthy city looks like . Chances are their answer will be something like : A healthy city is one where most of the people eat well , exercise a bunch and live long lives free of chronic disease . And that&amp;rsquo ; s probably true . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But what if we have it backwards ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What if a healthy city is one that makes people **25;29965;TOOLONG to be healthier ? A town , a community , that inspires its citizens naturally to make the kinds of healthy lifestyle choices that endless nagging fails to do ? What if it&amp;rsquo ; s the environment &amp;mdash ; which includes the people we surround ourselves with &amp;mdash ; that makes us healthier , not the other way around ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And what if Ottawa could be the healthiest city of them all ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;29992;TOOLONG ... title= " Jake Cole of the Sierra Club Canada Foundation , left , and columnist Brigitte Pellerin enjoy the pedestrian pathways in Ottawa . " alt= " Jake Cole of the Sierra Club Canada Foundation , left , and columnist Brigitte Pellerin enjoy the pedestrian pathways in Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;30028;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425114 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to the most recent &lt;a href= " http : **34;30058;TOOLONG ... of Ottawa 's Health&lt;/a&gt; report by Ottawa Public Health , 67 per cent of us think our health is either very good or excellent , which is more than the average in the rest of Ontario ( henceforth affectionately dubbed the ROO ) , at 61 per cent . But one-third of Ottawa adults are overweight while 23 per cent are obese , which is similar to rates in the ROO. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the other hand , our life expectancy at birth , at 81.9 years for men and 85.2 years for women , compares favourably to ROO numbers ( 80.5 and 84.5 years , respectively ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So we&amp;rsquo ; re pretty good . But we can be better . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For starters &amp;ndash ; &amp;nbsp ; and it&amp;rsquo ; s no surprise &amp;ndash ; Ottawa Public Health thinks we should eat more veggies and move about more . More than one-third of Ottawa adults don&amp;rsquo ; t meet the exercise guideline of 150 minutes a week ; 59 per cent of youth spend more than two hours a day sucking a screen ; and nearly half our kids fail to get eight hours of sleep at night . Not a recipe for living to a ripe old age . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is estimated that fully 70 per cent of premature deaths ( that&amp;rsquo ; s what we call dying before the age of 75 ) can be avoided by preventing the onset of disease or by delaying the progression of disease with proper detection and treatment . We have doctors and hospitals for the second part . It&amp;rsquo ; s the first one , prevention , that you and I could work on more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How ? Maybe what we need is to become something of a &amp;ldquo ; Blue Zone . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This has nothing to do with political parties ( whew ! ) . Rather , it&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;30094;TOOLONG ... a book&lt;/a&gt; of the same name by Dan Buettner , identifying five areas in the world where people live long , healthy lives , and where spry centenarians are more common than elsewhere : Okinawa , Japan ; Sardinia , Italy ; Nicoya , Costa Rica ; Ikaria , Greece ; and Loma Linda , California . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30129;TOOLONG ... title= " The beachfront in the village of Therma , on Ikaria , Greece . Ikaria is considered one of the healthiest places on Earth . Ottawa is n't Greece , but we have lots of advantages that could make us all healthier . " alt= " The beachfront in the village of Therma , on Ikaria , Greece . Ikaria is considered one of the healthiest places on Earth . Ottawa is n't Greece , but we have lots of advantages that could make us all healthier . " data-portal-copyright= " Howard Fagen " **28;30165;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425112 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buettner identified nine principles that contribute to making Blue Zones the healthiest areas to live in : &amp;ldquo ; move naturally&amp;rdquo ; ; &amp;ldquo ; purpose&amp;rdquo ; ; &amp;ldquo ; down shift&amp;rdquo ; ( i.e. reduce stress ) ; &amp;ldquo ; 80 per cent rule&amp;rdquo ; ( stop eating before you&amp;rsquo ; re full ) ; &amp;ldquo ; plant slant&amp;rdquo ; ( eat less meat ) ; &amp;ldquo ; wine @ 5&amp;rdquo ; ( moderate drinking ) ; &amp;ldquo ; belong&amp;rdquo ; ( faith/community ) ; &amp;ldquo ; loved ones first&amp;rdquo ; ( family ) ; and &amp;ldquo ; right tribe&amp;rdquo ; ( social network ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Blue Zones project , inspired by the book , aims to help cities apply those nine health principles to their own environment , as a way to help citizens become healthier . And it appears to work . Fort Worth , Texas , decided to try turning blue a few years ago . It went from nearly dead-last in the Gallup-Sharecare Well-Being index of American cities in 2014 , to 58th out of 185 in 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;30195;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s the kind of progress that makes people pay attention &amp;ndash ; including in the Calgary suburb of Airdrie , which aims to become the first Canadian city certified as a Blue Zone . Airdrie &amp;amp ; Area Health Cooperative&amp;rsquo ; s Stacey Lush said the certification is a few years out , but &amp;ldquo ; Airdrie is ready to put the boots to the pavement and work hard to get healthy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa : If that&amp;rsquo ; s not a challenge , I don&amp;rsquo ; t know what is. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What would we need to make our city &amp;ldquo ; bluer&amp;rdquo ; &amp;ndash ; or at any rate healthier ? Could we apply some of the Blue Zones principles to help us lead happier , healthier lives ? What would that look like ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Grab your carrot sticks and sensible shoes and we&amp;rsquo ; ll take a stroll in the fresh air over the next few weeks , as we discuss how to make our region as healthy as it can be . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **32;30231;TOOLONG Pellerin&lt;/strong&gt; is an Ottawa writer who wants this city to be the healthiest in Canada . Her occasional series will appear on Tuesdays and Fridays . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;30265;TOOLONG ... " alt= " A file photo of part of the General campus of The Ottawa Hospital . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;30488;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1406167 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anxiety levels are high among some physicians at The Ottawa Hospital as it transitions to a new electronic health record system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One doctor , who contacted this newspaper , said some physicians feel they were not adequately trained before the June 1 launch and are not receiving the support they need . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We feel abandoned . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a statement , the hospital&amp;rsquo ; s director of communications and engagement , Kate Eggins , said that 98 per cent of physicians had training . Additional training and support were also provided . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We take concerns of staff and physicians seriously and we encourage them to speak openly to colleagues and management when they have a concern . we have always acknowledged that moving from a paper system to a digital one would require substantial change to workflows , processes and procedures . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor also said the number of glitches in the system is much higher than physicians had expected and will take months , not weeks , to correct . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am shocked by the amount of glitches . The system is in relative chaos right now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Hospital is one of six Eastern Ontario health organizations to adopt the Epic electronic health record system . Several other Ontario hospitals , including CHEO , had previously adopted the U.S.-developed system that is used around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Alan Karovitch , an internal medicine doctor who is vice-president of the medical staff association at The Ottawa Hospital , agreed that the transition has been stressful. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People have to understand this is the biggest process change any of us will see in our careers . Change management is hard . But if you keep your eye on the prize and contextualize it , you realize this is OK. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;30518;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;30560;TOOLONG ... woman 's appendix bursts during lengthy wait to be admitted to **29;30595;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;30626;TOOLONG ... record system brings some delays , frustrations at The Ottawa **29;30661;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Karovitch said he has been well supported throughout the process and , although there have been frustrating days , he has always felt supported , confident about patient care and that things are improving . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it is good for patient care &amp;mdash ; no more sticky notes on charts , no more faxes . We are getting out of that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor who spoke to this newspaper and a second physician said that they are concerned about potential errors and delays during the glitch-prone rollout of the Epic system , as well as expanding wait lists for appointments and non-urgent surgery . The doctor said he had issued eight &amp;ldquo ; tickets&amp;rdquo ; to report issues with the system and has not even received an acknowledgment about when they will be fixed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This newspaper has been told that 10,000 tickets &amp;mdash ; representing issues with the system &amp;mdash ; were filed within the first two days after its launch . He agreed to speak on the condition of anonymity , saying he feared for his job . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The transition is adding significantly to physicians&amp;rsquo ; workloads , the doctor said , tripling the amount of work being done in some cases . That means they are spending more time looking at computers than with patients and is slowing things down . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In some cases it has led to delays in transferring patients from one campus to another . It is also increasing the risk of errors in scheduling tests and medication . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The irony is that so many things don&amp;rsquo ; t work , we have the worst of both worlds . We still have to fax things , &amp;rdquo ; said the doctor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another physician said the new system has resulted in chaos and low morale and set the stage for dangerous situations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Duplicate bookings , the physician said , are common . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A woman who was taken to the General campus by ambulance on June 2 , the day after Epic launched , experienced that . She said she was on her way to having a second , unnecessary , CT scan before she questioned why and health workers discovered it was an error . This newspaper has heard other reports of duplicate tests being done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hospital has added extra staff to help with the transition and has brought in superusers to help in the early weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of those super users , Bassil Abuzeid , a recent Carleton University economics graduate who was trained to help support staff learning the new system , said he understands the transition is difficult . &amp;ldquo ; But from the superuser point of view , the system is not as bad as people think . It is meant to be more convenient for the staff . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abuzeid said that in his experience staff members were catching on to the new system quickly . The result will be an improved information flow , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think the system creates transparency between the departments and is much more convenient . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The move to fully digitalize health records has been slow in Canada , with doctors offices and hospitals relying on faxes and handwritten notes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eggins , said some transition slowdowns were anticipated and the hospital has taken steps to minimize them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She also said a fully digital health records system is necessary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The era of the fax machine has to go . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor said physicians were given a chance to test the system and familiarize themselves with it ahead of time , but there were so many problems with that &amp;ldquo ; play space&amp;rdquo ; that they got little practice , which has left them feeling unprepared . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Under the new system , doctors and nurses are responsible for many of the tasks previously done by others &amp;mdash ; including scheduling and ordering medication . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some positions will been cut as a result , although the hospital says it will retrain people whose positions are made redundant . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The doctor conceded that some things are improving but said it feels like the complex new system has been rolled out too quickly and with too little support for those using it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The situation is discouraging and stressful for many physicians , who feel they are spending more time with the computer than with sick patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are really dedicated and invested in good patient care . This has really been a big blow to that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;30692;TOOLONG " **34;30721;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;30757;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;31072;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1413908 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Staff at the hospital seemed preoccupied by the new patient record system throughout her multi-day stay there , the woman recalled . This newspaper has agreed not to reveal her name . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everybody in the emergency department was walking around talking about how terrible the new system was and how they couldn&amp;rsquo ; t give proper care to patients because they were spending time trying to figure out the new online system . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Hospital was among six Eastern Ontario health care providers that switched to the online patient record system Epic beginning on June 1 . The system will allow health care providers to share patient information instantly and allow patients to access their own records . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa Hospital chief of staff Virginia Roth said she believes the $87-million , multi-hospital system will save lives by minimizing errors and allowing for faster , more accurate care among other things . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This newspaper has heard from several patients who have described confusion getting access to records and other glitches in the days since the new system was adopted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Teams from the U.S. company that designs and sells the information system have been working in the hospitals during the transition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hospital would not comment on an individual case but said staff in the emergency department triage all patients . The introduction of the new information system has not changed the way that patients are triaged &amp;ldquo ; or how patients in need of emergency care are seen . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spokesperson Michaela Schreiter said staff and physician numbers in emergency departments were increased &amp;ldquo ; to support the transition to the new digital health information system , to ensure that patients continue to receive the best care possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;31102;TOOLONG ... title= " A woman whose appendix burst while she waited four hours to be admitted to The Ottawa Hospital earlier this month poses for a photo in her home on Saturday , June 15 , 2019. " alt= " A woman whose appendix burst while she waited four hours to be admitted to The Ottawa Hospital earlier this month poses for a photo in her home on Saturday , June 15 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Patrick Doyle " **28;31138;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1413903 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The woman said her ordeal began over the weekend . On Saturday , June 1 , she thought she had food poisoning . By midday Sunday , she had severe abdominal pain . Her husband called an ambulance Sunday evening because she couldn&amp;rsquo ; t physically get into the car to go to the hospital . The paramedics assisting her &amp;ldquo ; immediately thought it was my appendix . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She had to wait four hours to get assessed or get pain medication she said , although the emergency department did not seem to be unusually busy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After she was admitted , blood was taken and she had a quick ultrasound , but was told they couldn&amp;rsquo ; t see much and would have to wait until morning for a more sensitive ultrasound . The surgery took an hour and a half , longer than expected because infection had spread throughout her abdomen , she was told . She was also told that her recovery would be a little bit longer than with a less complex case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The woman said that during her stay in the hospital staff told her everything was slower because they were spending a lot of time on computers dealing with the new records system or meeting with company reps to have questions answered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By Wednesday , when she had started feeling better , a nurse told her about another appendix case with a very different timeline . A patient had come in that morning with appendicitis , was rushed into surgery and had gone home at 2 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What was the difference with my case that I had to wait compared to the woman who showed up and was taken care of quickly ? What happened ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa Hospital said it does not comment on individual cases but thoroughly reviews all cases where a concern is brought to its attention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;31168;TOOLONG ... family braces for NCR ruling , suing son 's accused killer&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;31203;TOOLONG ... divest of wagon wheels , balaclavas and G7 brooms at GCSurplus sale&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;31238;TOOLONG ... promise to expand universal health care , starting with national drug plan&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneMon , 17 Jun 2019 21:20:14 **45;31273;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenWomenHemming and St. Denis : World will pay a price if we do n't resolve the menstruation taboohttp : **35;31320;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;31357;TOOLONG ... title= " A man walks by a wall painting about female menstruation on the Menstrual Hygiene Day in Guwahati , India , on May 28 , 2019. " alt= " A man walks by a wall painting about female menstruation on the Menstrual Hygiene Day in Guwahati , India , on May 28 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " DAVID TALUKDAR " **28;31393;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1408778 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The start of menstruation is a sign of reproductive health and nature 's rite of passage from childhood to womanhood . It has a huge influence on a girl 's sense of identity and self-worth . However , in the developing world , where adequate sanitation facilities ( separate latrines for girls and boys , for example ) are rare , menstruation can be a barrier to education , employment , equity , empowerment and a sense of self-worth. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Unfortunately in too many cultures , it is also a taboo subject shrouded in shame and secrecy . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;In fact , UNICEF data show that one-third of schools in the developing world do not even have adequate facilities . In sub-Saharan Africa , this figure is nearly 50 per cent . This can open girls up to ridicule , taunting or even sexual harassment . In an attempt to find the only privacy available to attend to their needs and clean themselves up , some wait until darkness to go in the woods , where they risk being sexually assaulted or attacked . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;There is also an issue of silence . Most girls have very little information about what is happening to their bodies . According to WASH United , one girl in three in South Asia knew nothing about menstruation before the onset of her first period . Many more have only a vague idea of what they are supposed to do during menstruation . In Sri Lanka , one study found that only 41 per cent of girls had good overall knowledge of menstrual health management . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;More commonly , girls will simply refuse to go to school during menstruation . If they miss five days of school a month ( 25 per cent ) , they can fall further and further behind until the situation can seem hopeless and they drop out . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Girls who drop out of school tend to have lower earning potential , more menial jobs , be married off earlier and be subjected to more frequent partner violence . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Even for the lucky ones who stay in school and get jobs , they may still have to deal with inadequate facilities for their menstruation needs . This can translate into absenteeism , lack of promotion opportunities and ultimately , forgone income . For those who start their own businesses , the problem is compounded by having to leave their business unattended several times a day , in an attempt to find the few private spots available to attend to their needs . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;If you consider all the days a woman menstruates in her lifetime , it amounts to years . Embarrassed or ostracized , she may cut herself off from school , work , social interaction and being a full participant in her community . This is like serving a sentence of years of house arrest . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Most important is to understand that in failing to resolve this situation , we are all paying a price . With every one-per-cent increase in the proportion of women with secondary education , a country 's &lt;a href= " https : **33;31423;TOOLONG ... class= " s2 " &gt;per capita income grows by 0.3 per **27;31458;TOOLONG every year . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;With strong evidence that poor water , sanitation and hygiene facilities affect maternal and reproductive health outcomes , the availability of facilities for appropriate menstruation management should be recognized as a human rights issue . It is high time this situation received priority attention . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Women Deliver is the world 's largest gathering on the health , rights and well-being of women and girls . Canada hosted the global conference in Vancouver recently . At the conference , the prime minister announced the government will invest $14 billion over 10 years to promote the health and rights of women and girls around the world . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;A smart investment for some of this money would be to help equip all schools and workplaces with basic separate sanitation facilities and culturally appropriate hygiene products . Anything less is an affront to women 's and girls ' inherent dignity as a human being . The province says it will cut $200 million dollars province-wide by the end of the 2020/21 fiscal year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Egli said there is little doubt Ottawa will be forced to make cuts to some of the services currently provided by Ottawa Public Health &amp;mdash ; whose work ranges from immunization to water testing , restaurant inspections , mental health services , mother and infant care and much more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And Egli expressed frustration that the information being given to municipalities and public health units remains sketchy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Council on Wednesday voted unanimously to have Mayor Jim Watson write to the provincial government to express support for Ottawa Public Health and call on the government to maintain OPH 's mandate for health protection , promotion and prevention . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Council is also asking that the province maintain the current cost-sharing scheme of 75/25 between the province and the city . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Medical officer of health Vera Etches said there has been little information from the province about what will happen over the next year before the health units are merged . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Etches said OPH is already efficient from an administrative perspective , since the City of Ottawa does the " back office " work . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province has confirmed that it will slash the number of health units in Ontario from the current 35 to 10 . Toronto has been told it will remain a stand-alone public health unit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;32288;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;32324;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Ottawa has no information about whether it will be grouped with other area health units to form a regional health unit and what that will mean to budgets and organizational structure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The thing that makes me scratch my head is that ( the province ) announced they are going from 35 to 10 , but they don&amp;rsquo ; t seem to be able to tell us what those 10 regions are , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; It does seem odd that you can make an announcement and yet not be able to identify where those zones are . That is going to be so important . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa Public Health has unique challenges , he said , including that it serves a bilingual population , living in a diverse geographic area that includes indigenous and refugee populations with their own unique health concerns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa also has a significant overdose issue and drug-using population . Ottawa Public Health has long been involved with harm reduction services . The province recently cut funding to a supervised consumption site run by Ottawa Public Health . It continues to operate , now funded by the city , which will add to the burden the city faces when more public health costs are downloaded onto it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; For an organization as large as public health , this is a significant shift change , &amp;rdquo ; Egli said . &amp;ldquo ; The province has indicated they are going to consult starting very soon . I am hoping that is accurate , there is a lot to be discussed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province says by regionalizing public health it will modernize and improve the services provided and allow for administrative efficiencies .
