Elizabeth Payne , Jon WillingWed , 24 Apr 2019 21:19:04 **45;32564;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenPoliticsWomenThe drug resistant fungus that is putting health officials on alerthttp : **35;32611;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;32648;TOOLONG ... title= " File photo . " alt= " File photo . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;32684;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1059346 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Winnipeg resident returned from India seriously ill with a brain abscess and a chronic ear infection . The unidentified patient , who had undergone dental surgery in India , was infected with a drug-resistant form of bacteria . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that was not all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The 64-year-old is now recognized as Canada&amp;rsquo ; s Patient Zero for an emerging new threat that has hospitals across the country on alert &amp;mdash ; multi-drug resistant Candida auris . The fungal infection was found in the patient&amp;rsquo ; s ear when &amp;ldquo ; cheesy&amp;rdquo ; fluid was drained from it and tested . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Candida auris can spread from patient to patient and can live on skin , walls , curtains , handles , cellphones and other surfaces . The risk of infection in otherwise healthy patients is relatively low ; Candida auris can colonize the skin of patients with no visible impact . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in people with suppressed immune systems , it can cause a serious infection , enter their bloodstream and even be deadly . In some countries where it is common , mortality rates for those with invasive Candida auris are higher than 50 per cent . What is more , treatment options are limited or non-existent , and it is difficult to eradicate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that is worrisome to hospitals , because many have significant populations of patients with suppressed immune systems &amp;mdash ; those undergoing cancer treatment or transplant patients , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;32714;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;32756;TOOLONG ... technology sanitizes smart phones in hospitals with UV **26;32791;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; What has health officials most concerned about Candida auris is how difficult it is to identify , treat and contain &amp;mdash ; especially its persistence . A recent New York Times story described a hospital having to rip out floor and ceiling tiles in order to eradicate it from an infected patient&amp;rsquo ; s room . It can also live on reusable medical equipment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it is something that all hospitals are worried about , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Julianne Kus , a clinical microbiologist at Public Health Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada has seen only 19 cases of Candida auris . Those cases include a cluster in British Columbia and five unrelated cases in southern Ontario . None of the Ontario cases was multi-drug resistant . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The authors of the paper about Patient Zero , who was diagnosed in May 2017 , note that multi-drug resistant Candida auris was first identified in Japan in 2009 and has , since then , become an emerging global threat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fact that it behaves differently than other infectious threats &amp;mdash ; even clinging to synthetic surfaces and forming a biofilm &amp;mdash ; makes it so hard to eradicate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Humans are colonized with many strains of Candida , notes Kus . Sometimes , they can cause infections such as vaginal yeast infections and athlete&amp;rsquo ; s foot that are more a nuisance than serious infections . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What makes Candida auris different , though , is that it can be spread from person to person , similar to bacteria and viruses . &amp;ldquo ; Typically , we have never really been concerned about fungi or yeast spreading from person to person , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What is more , Candida auris appears to be able to spread from a person into the environment and back to a person . When the strain is multi-drug resistant and resistant to disinfectant cleaning products , it quickly can become a nightmare for hospitals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It seems to be acting a little bit differently . We haven&amp;rsquo ; t really thought about fungi this way before , &amp;rdquo ; said Kus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another complicating factor is that treatment for fungal infections is limited and anti-fungal drugs can be harmful so must be used judiciously . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Earlier this year , Public Health Ontario published a document guiding health officials in the province on how to approach the emerging health threat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among other advice : Rooms of patients or residents colonized with C. auris should be cleaned and disinfected daily , or even twice a day and medical equipment used on the patient should not be used on anyone else . C. auris can spread between patients as quickly as four hours and patients can even catch it from being in a room previously occupied by a patient . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The verdict is still out on the impact of ultraviolet light and other disinfectant procedures . There is some evidence that hydrogen peroxide vapour and ultraviolet light can reduce levels of environmental contamination with C. auris , but whether this will result in reduced transmission has not been confirmed , said Kus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Facilities that are using ultraviolet light or hydrogen peroxide vapour should prioritize rooms housing C. auris patients for the disinfection , but the room should first be cleaned and disinfected using standard procedures , she said , which includes the use of bleach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been no cases in Ottawa , but hospitals are prepared for cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kus says it is important to draw attention to multi-disease resistant infections such as Candida auris. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It highlights that we are still vulnerable to infectious disease . We will not be able to rely on antibiotics forever . &amp;rdquo ; Kus noted that the World Health Organization has named drug resistance as a leading health threat . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Should ( Canadians ) be worried ? In Canada we are doing a pretty good job so far in containing it . We haven&amp;rsquo ; t had the same experience as is happening in the States . I would say from the community level , I would not worry about it . But it is a good thing to talk about . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;32819;TOOLONG " **34;32848;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;32884;TOOLONG ... dog kills wild turkey downtown&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;32919;TOOLONG ... erupts as LeBlanc sentenced to 15 years for second-degree murder&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;32955;TOOLONG ... River expected to peak for a second time in May -- and it could be worse , officials warn&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneWed , 24 Apr 2019 11:42:51 **45;32990;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenNew technology sanitizes smart phones in hospitals with UV lighthttp : **35;33037;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;33074;TOOLONG ... title= " The Montfort Hospital is using a CleanSlate UV machine to kill bacteria on devices like cellphones. " alt= " The Montfort Hospital is using a CleanSlate UV machine to kill bacteria on devices like cellphones. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;33110;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1384996 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;33140;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Montfort Hospital features a microwave-like device in its front lobby that cleans your phone of dangerous bacteria using ultraviolet light . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The device , called CleanSlate UV , was created in order to combat antibiotic resistant bacteria born in hospitals and commonly referred to as superbugs . The illnesses they cause vary from minor infections to severe diseases . Common strains include C. difficile and MRSA. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MRSA complications are sometimes as minor as small skin infections or as serious as pneumonia . Similarly , C. difficile can result in symptoms ranging from diarrhea to life-threatening inflammation of the colon . Both bacteria can live for weeks on hard surfaces , such as cellphones or tablets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By harnessing a specific wavelength of ultra-violet light that is refracted in the chamber of the device , CleanSlate UV attacks bacteria on mobile phones and other electronic devices so that it can no longer reproduce . The process takes 20 seconds &amp;ndash ; allowing time to use the hand-sanitizer located conveniently next to it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There have been numerous studies that show that one in four mobile hospital devices will have a pathogen bacteria on it , &amp;rdquo ; said Taylor Mann , co-founder of CleanSlate UV. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a growing concern , as the number of mobile devices used in hospitals is steadily increasing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Hospitals put a huge focus on hand hygiene , &amp;rdquo ; Mann explained . &amp;ldquo ; But it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t make sense to wash your hands and then immediately touch a dirty device . Hospitals are deploying these very expensive touch-screen products to make their routine easier . &amp;rdquo ; Sanitizing the mobile devices is often not thought of until after they are purchased . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;33176;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;33218;TOOLONG ... drug resistant fungus that is putting health officials on **26;33253;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The CleanSlate UV provides a non-chemical way of killing bacteria . Chemical disinfectants that are typically used to clean mobile devices are harsh , he said , and can wear devices down over time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t actually physically polish the device , &amp;rdquo ; Mann explained . &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re able to eliminate bacteria on the surfaces without damaging devices , and in a way that completely removes human error . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Montfort partnered with CleanSlate UV as part of a two-phase pilot project to conduct research on how the public can use CleanSlate. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to make it very quick , very easy , for the public to sanitize their devices at the same time as they are sanitizing their hands , &amp;rdquo ; Mann said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jos&amp;eacute ; e Shymanski , manager of the Infection Control Department at Montfort , said she&amp;rsquo ; s happy to see an increase in people washing their hands when they use the device . The CleanSlate&amp;rsquo ; s most recent location , by the line for the coffee shop in the front lobby , saw the most public usage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We saw an increase to 32 times a day , &amp;rdquo ; she said , as compared to the 20 times a day it was used when it was located by the front entrance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The second phase of the pilot project involves technology that is not yet on the market . By putting small identification tags on hospital devices , the CleanSlate automatically records which devices are cleaned and when . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It all adds up to them having more information so that they can protect their staff and patients , &amp;rdquo ; Mann said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mann expects that the tagging technology will be available to purchase sometime this summer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;33281;TOOLONG ... flood of 2019 : ' Severe flood levels ' now expected&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;33316;TOOLONG ... Transpo retires route number in Westboro crash&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;33351;TOOLONG ... Obama is coming to speak in Ottawa&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Erica EndemannWed , 24 Apr 2019 11:40:03 **45;33386;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **26;33433;TOOLONG policies in Canada and beyond : What 's working and what needs to changehttp : **34;33461;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;33497;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Zachary Kaminsky , the chair of suicide prevention , poses for a photo at the Royal in Ottawa Tuesday Feb 6 , 2018. " alt= " Dr. Zachary Kaminsky , the chair of suicide prevention , poses for a photo at the Royal in Ottawa Tuesday Feb 6 , 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;33533;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382531 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Between 70 and 80 people take their own lives in Ottawa every year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Simon Hatcher , a psychiatrist and researcher in clinical epidemiology at The Ottawa Hospital Research Institute , said he believes it&amp;rsquo ; s possible to reduce that number by 20 per cent within five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s been done before . It&amp;rsquo ; s not rocket science , &amp;rdquo ; he says , pointing to the example of the Nuremberg model , a protocol first used in Germany which has now spread all over Europe and beyond . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m sure it can be reduced . We can do better . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suicide is considered a public health issue . But Ottawa Public Health has not set a target for reducing suicide in the city . Even the issue of setting targets is controversial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not sure setting a target will help . What will help is setting a collaborative approach , &amp;rdquo ; said Ben Leikin , supervisor of Ottawa Public Health&amp;rsquo ; s mental health team and co-chair of Suicide Prevention Ottawa , which recently merged with the Ottawa Suicide Prevention Coalition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s also important to consider whether a reduction in the number of suicide deaths and attempts would continue over time . There are some environmental factors , such as natural disasters or large-scale layoffs for example , that can&amp;rsquo ; t be controlled and have an effect on the suicide rate , says Leikin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Leikin said while he applauds those who have set a target for suicide reduction , Ottawa has not . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Any evidence that exists out there , we&amp;rsquo ; re open to it . Let&amp;rsquo ; s not reinvent the wheel . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been a number of strategies touted to prevent suicide . Here are a few of them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;33563;TOOLONG ... title= " Psychologist Dr. Antoon Leenaars. " alt= " Psychologist Dr. Antoon Leenaars. " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;33599;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382530 " /&gt; **28;33629;TOOLONG **33;33659;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; This &amp;ldquo ; continuous improvement model , &amp;rdquo ; first implemented in health-care systems in the U.S. , works on the idea that people who have contact with the health-care system can be prevented from taking their own lives . There are three main pillars : a direct approach to suicidal behaviours , continual improvement of the quality and safety of care processes and an organizational commitment to the aspirational goal of zero suicides. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the measures : removing the lethal means of suicide ; training a competent and caring workforce ; using screening to determine who is at risk of suicide ; ensuring all patients at risk are closely followed ; treating suicidal ideation independent of the patient&amp;rsquo ; s diagnosis and transitioning patients with &amp;ldquo ; warm hand-offs and supportive contact . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; The Henry Ford Health System , a non-profit health-care provider in the Detroit area that includes eight hospitals , 48 medical centres and 13 walk-in clinics , has often been named as an example of good outcomes . Henry Ford , which calls the protocol &amp;ldquo ; perfect depression care , &amp;rdquo ; introduced it in 2001 . In a study published in 2007 , Henry Ford reported a 75 per cent reduction in the suicide rate . There were actually zero suicides in the Henry Ford system for an 18-month stretch between 2009-2010. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The model has moved into Europe and Canada . The first Zero Suicide campaign in Canada was unveiled in May 2016 at the St. Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s Healthcare Foundation in London , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The model is not without controversy in the mental health community . Hatcher says there is one major problem with translating the zero suicide model : three-quarters of people who die by suicide have no contact with mental health services in the year leading to their death . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s a lot of rhetoric about it , but not a lot of evidence , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; It irritated a lot of clinicians by calling it Zero Suicide . It&amp;rsquo ; s like going into intensive care and saying , &amp;lsquo ; There will be zero deaths . &amp;rsquo ; As a psychiatrist , I am responsible for getting ( my patients ) the best treatment possible . The implication here is that we are responsible for a death by suicide . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Zero Suicide works for hospitals and health-care networks , not for regions and jurisdictions , said Leikin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is also debate over whether zero is an attainable goal . Zero Suicide calls it an &amp;ldquo ; aspirational challenge and practical framework for system-wide transformation . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we should shoot for Zero Suicide . But it will never happen . There will always be people with unbearable angst . Our task is to work toward it , &amp;rdquo ; says psychologist Dr. Antoon Leenaars , the former president of the Canadian Association for Suicide Prevention . &lt;/p&gt; **27;33694;TOOLONG Nuremberg **31;33723;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; The Nuremberg Alliance Against Depression was initiated in 2001 as a community-based model project in the German city of Nuremberg , which has about 500,000 residents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; Called a &amp;ldquo ; multi-level intervention , &amp;rdquo ; it&amp;rsquo ; s a four-level approach that engages partners from parents to police officers to family physicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; First level : General practitioners and pediatricians are invited to educational workshops on how to recognize and treat depression in the primary care setting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Second level : An awareness campaign for the general public is aimed at improving knowledge and reducing stigma . Campaigns have included posters , advertising spots in movie theatres , brochures , public events and websites . Local organizers are also urged to develop a relationship with the media to prompt discussion , and to set guidelines on coverage to prevent copycat suicides. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Third level : Patients in high risk groups &amp;mdash ; such as teens in crisis &amp;mdash ; are given &amp;ldquo ; emergency cards&amp;rdquo ; guaranteeing direct access to professional help . Local self-help groups are provided with expert advice and partnerships are created with patient associations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fourth level : Workshops are held with groups such as health-care professionals , counsellors , clergy and police officers . There are also information sessions for parents , teachers and youth workers and prevention programs in schools . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The program also tracks data on &amp;ldquo ; core indicators&amp;rdquo ; such as number of suicides , number of suicide attempts , public opinion , attitudes , knowledge and media coverage of suicides. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; A lot of other jurisdictions have adopted it . After suicidal behaviour was reduced by 20 per cent in Nuremberg , 18 partners in 17 European countries established the &lt;a href= " http : //www.eaad.net/ " &gt;European Alliance Against Depression&lt;/a&gt; in 2004 . There are now about 100 regional networks in the alliance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Mental Health Commission of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **33;33756;TOOLONG ... of Hope&lt;/a&gt; suicide prevention program , which has been adopted by communities in Labrador , Newfoundland and New Brunswick , has some parallels , but the European Alliance has stricter protocols . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;33791;TOOLONG ... title= " NDP MP Charlie Angus stands in the House of Commons during question period on Parliament Hill in Ottawa on June 9 , 2015. " alt= " NDP MP Charlie Angus stands in the House of Commons during question period on Parliament Hill in Ottawa on June 9 , 2015. " data-portal-copyright= " FRED CHARTRAND " **28;33827;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 794461 " /&gt; &lt;div class= " mceTemp " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; **25;33857;TOOLONG national **34;33884;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; More than 20 countries have a national strategy for suicide prevention . Canada has a federal framework , but not a strategy , and that needs to change , argues the Centre for Suicide Prevention , a branch of the Canadian Mental Health Association . The Canadian Association for Suicide Prevention has also been calling for a national strategy for more than 20 years and &lt;a href= " https : **31;33920;TOOLONG " &; ... a blueprint&lt;/a&gt; in 2004 and an update in 2009. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada is the only G8 country without a national suicide prevention strategy and one of the few industrialized countries that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t have one , according to the Centre for Suicide Prevention . The U.S. , Australia , Japan , Scotland , England and Finland all have working national policies in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The centre says research shows that a strategy must address suicide directly and have its own prevention strategy , outside the broad umbrella of mental health , &amp;ldquo ; otherwise there is a danger that the prevention message will be diluted , ignored , or lost altogether . &amp;rdquo ; The strategy also can&amp;rsquo ; t be the responsibility of a single government department , but must be addressed by all government departments and sectors affected , including the community , workplace , schools , families , the criminal justice system and education . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; Finland was one of the first countries to introduce a national strategy in the 1980s , and the suicide rate there dropped by nine per cent over 10 years , according to the Centre for Suicide Prevention . Scotland introduced a strategy in 2002 and saw an 18 per cent reduction in suicides by 2012. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jackie Doyle-Price was named the United Kingdom&amp;rsquo ; s first Suicide Prevention Minister in October 2018 . Her role oversees a strategy that includes a focus on how social media and technology , such as predictive analytics and artificial intelligence , can help identify suicide risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; England&amp;rsquo ; s strategy was introduced in 2012 . In a progress report published in January , Doyle-Price said the suicide rate in England was at its lowest in seven years . &amp;ldquo ; However , these rates can change over time , &amp;rdquo ; she noted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Achieving a low rate and maintaining it over time is an eternal problem in suicide prevention . In the U.S. , which has a national strategy , the suicide rate increased 24 per cent between 1999 and 2014 , from 10.5 to 13 suicides for every 100,000 people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; NDP MP Charlie Angus introduced a parliamentary motion for a national suicide prevention &amp;ldquo ; action plan&amp;rdquo ; earlier this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to put language to this that says this is an epidemic , that it&amp;rsquo ; s causing devastation , &amp;rdquo ; says Angus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It also has to be a made-in-Canada plan that recognizes the realities of vulnerable groups in Canadian society , such as Indigenous Peoples , and the relationship between the provinces and federal government when it comes to health policy , he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among other measures , &lt;a href= " https : **32;33953;TOOLONG ... motion , M-174&lt;/a&gt; , calls for a national public health monitoring program , national standards of training for those who work with vulnerable populations and an annual report to Parliament , which would include data on progress for the previous year . &amp;ldquo ; If we&amp;rsquo ; re not diminishing the numbers of deaths , then the plan is not working , &amp;rdquo ; said Angus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The motion is up for second debate on May 2 . If it passes , Angus said Canada would be obligated to put a number of steps in place , including identifying groups at elevated risk and introducing strategies that would prevent suicide among these groups within 18 months . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was shocked at the level of suicide among middle-aged men , &amp;rdquo ; said Angus. &amp;ldquo ; When a large industrial plant goes down , we need to have a plan to raise awareness ( about suicide ) . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; **37;33987;TOOLONG the biology of **43;34026;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What is it ? &lt;/strong&gt; Scientists are delving into the complicated interplay between genetics and environment and the role each plays in suicide risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For example , molecular biologist Zachary Kaminsky&amp;rsquo ; s research hit the news in 2014 after he and colleagues at Johns Hopkins University in Baltimore discovered a chemical alteration in a gene called SKA2 linked to stress reduction . The finding suggested that doctors could identify suicide risk and even prevent suicide through a blood or saliva test . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it&amp;rsquo ; s complicated , said Kaminsky , who is now the DIFD Mach-Gaensslen Chair in Suicide Prevention Research at The Royal&amp;rsquo ; s Institute of Mental Health Research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaminsky did a study of about 925 soldiers deployed to Afghanistan . A blood test identified a variant of SKA2 , correctly identifying five soldiers who had elevated scores for suicidal ideation. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the tests also found a high number of false positives &amp;mdash ; these soldiers tested positive , but they did not have suicidal thoughts . That brings up the question of identifying people who are not at risk . There&amp;rsquo ; s also the problem of identifying those who are at risk , but not being able to do anything to help them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to understand if we will cause a problem that we have no solution for , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaminsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Does it work ? &lt;/strong&gt; Researchers are learning more and more about the interplay for genetics and the environment . But it&amp;rsquo ; s not as simple as administering a once-in-a-lifetime blood test to everyone to determine whether or not an individual is genetically vulnerable to suicidal thoughts . First of all , genes may be switched on or off by traumatic or stressful events . Biological systems are adaptive to experiences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not just genes . It&amp;rsquo ; s context , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaminsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the same time , vulnerability to suicide risk is believed to be determined by a complex interplay of genetic factors . &amp;ldquo ; Is there one gene that does it all ? If I said that , I would get ripped apart , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; In term of coming up with a magic bullet that works for everyone , it becomes very challenging . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he also has a lot of hope . At some point , a negative test result may conclude that someone is not at risk of suicide , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The more research we do , the more we understand about the biological systems that will lead to more treatment targets , &amp;rdquo ; said Kaminsky. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Treatments target systems we believe to be dysregulated with the hope of bringing them back in line . The more we know , the more we will be able to do this correctly . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He uses an analogy : A car owner notices the coolant is low , so he adds more . Then it runs low again , so he adds more . Adding more solved the problem for a time , but not the underlying problem . The mechanic figures out that the problem is the water pump . &amp;ldquo ; Once fixed , no more symptoms and no more treating the symptoms because the treatment was better . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are also promising new drug treatments for helping people in crisis . In March , the FDA approved a new depression treatment in the form of ketamine , which has been used as a painkiller , anesthetic and recreational drug , delivered as a nasal spray . A study of depressed patients at imminent risk of suicide in emergency rooms at 11 centres in the U.S. found the treatment resulted in significantly rapid improvement in depressive symptoms &amp;mdash ; including some measures of suicidal ideation. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt; &lt;strong&gt;FEATURE STORY&lt;/strong&gt; : &lt;a href= " https : **33;34071;TOOLONG ... Richards took her own life at 26 . She left behind devastated loved ones and a haunting question&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusThu , 18 Apr 2019 13:07:06 **45;34106;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **25;34153;TOOLONG Richards took her own life at 26 . She left behind devastated loved ones and a haunting questionhttp : **32;34180;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;34214;TOOLONG ... title= " Jennifer Richards and her son Simon pose with a photo of Lauren Richards in Ottawa Wednesday March 27 , 2019 . Lauren died Feb. 21. " alt= " Jennifer Richards and her son Simon pose with a photo of Lauren Richards in Ottawa Wednesday March 27 , 2019 . Lauren died Feb. 21. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;34250;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382524 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;34280;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Lauren Richards died on Feb. 21 by suicide . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The incident , outlined in cryptic terms by authorities , made the news because Richards died in a public place . We could tell you where and how , but the Canadian Psychiatric Association in its guidelines for journalists cautions against reporting on the details . Suicide contagion is real . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Richards ' name has never been released . But her family and friends say there is a story behind the few bare paragraphs in a press release , a tale of unbearable mental anguish and a question that still haunts them : what could be done to stop someone from dying when death is what they desire so fervently and seek so avidly ? &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; About 11 people die by suicide every day in Canada , according to the Canadian Association for Suicide Prevention . On average , another 200 Canadians attempt suicide every day . This is likely an underestimate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Based on data from the Canadian Community Health Survey , 14.7 per cent of Canadians have thought about suicide and 3.5 per cent have attempted suicide in their lifetime . Suicide is ranked as the ninth leading cause of death in Canada , but the second leading cause of death , after accidents , for young people between the ages of 15 and 34. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is a public health conundrum that has never been solved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34316;TOOLONG ... title= " Jennifer Richards and her son Simon pose with a photo of Lauren Richards in Ottawa Wednesday March 27 , 2019 . Lauren died Feb. 21. " alt= " Jennifer Richards and her son Simon pose with a photo of Lauren Richards in Ottawa Wednesday March 27 , 2019 . Lauren died Feb. 21. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;34352;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382524 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; About 6,000 people go the emergency rooms of the General and Civic campuses of The Ottawa Hospital every year seeking help for a mental health crisis . About half of these report having suicidal thoughts , says Dr. Simon Hatcher , a researcher at The Ottawa Hospital Research Institute . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards was 26 years old when she died and had been struggling with mental health issues since high school , say her friends and family . She had bulimia as a teen and attempted to take her own life many times . She swallowed pills and cut herself so often that she started to wear long sleeves all the time in her late teens . On at least two occasions , she needed stitches . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Only 12 days before her death , Richards posted a photo of herself on Instagram , pale and propped up by pillows . She is smiling faintly . The photo clearly shows the scars of self-inflicted wounds that line her upper arms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t it be great if we could talk about our mental health/illnesses or show our scars without judgment from others ? &amp;rdquo ; reads her post . &amp;ldquo ; If you have mental health problems , don&amp;rsquo ; t be afraid to talk about them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards was in unbearable mental pain . When her brother Simon heard that a woman had perished on the news , he immediately thought of his sister . There was a flurry of texts . Before long , 11 people were waiting at a friend&amp;rsquo ; s house , dreading the arrival of the police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I had a strong fear of getting a phone call like this . It was like my worst fears had been realized . It was the worst thing I could imagine , &amp;rdquo ; says Simon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34382;TOOLONG ... title= " Lauren Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019 . She was 26. " alt= " Lauren Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019 . She was 26. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;34418;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382528 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2017 , after Richards was discharged from hospital after yet another round of treatments , she was diagnosed with depression and possibly borderline personality disorder , although she did not meet the criteria for a formal diagnosis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Borderline personality disorder , known as BPD , is a condition characterized by fraught relationships , impulsiveness , extreme emotions and , sometimes , recurrent suicidal behaviour . Its hallmark is instability : an unsteady sense of self and emotions . People with BPD sometimes also suffer from depression , eating disorders and substance abuse . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To have BPD is to be on a constant roller coaster of emotions . Patients are often dismissed as needy , drama queens and attention-seekers , even by health professionals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Going to lose my mind , not because I have BPD , but because of all the stigma attached to it . PLEASE educate yourself before you judge me : ) &amp;rdquo ; Richards posted on Instagram a month before her death . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even though BPD affects only one to two per cent of the population , between five and 10 per cent of people who have the illness die by suicide . Most suicides are found in two major diagnoses : depression and BPD , says Dr. Antoon Leenaars , a Windsor-based psychologist and suicide researcher who is the former president of the Canadian Association for Suicide Prevention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They are constant fodder in the news because their behaviour is both perplexing and sometimes deadly to themselves . An inquest into the suicide death of Carleton University student Jason Simon heard in January that an emergency room psychiatrist suspected Simon had BPD . It was one of several diagnoses given to Justin St. Amour , whose suicide inquest heard earlier this month that he had planned to strip naked and freeze to death in a cemetery &amp;ldquo ; because I have more in common with the dead than the living . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People with borderline have a very fragile existence . Often they have volatile relationships , with impulsivity and a fear of real or imagined abandonment . They are difficult patients . They have very difficult lives , &amp;rdquo ; says Leenaars. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They create chaos . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard for them not to . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet patients describe the emotional pain they feel as real &amp;mdash ; and intolerable . The American suicidologist Edwin Shneidman called it &amp;ldquo ; psychache. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Suicide cries to the mind as a solution because life is constantly breaking into a thousand pieces , &amp;rdquo ; says Leenaars. &amp;ldquo ; They are always skating on thin ice . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34448;TOOLONG ... title= " Artwork by Lauren Richards . " alt= " Artwork by Lauren Richards . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;34484;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382522 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards may have been struggling , but she was also a resilient young woman with plans for her life , say her family and friends . She was a talented artist . Her favourite subjects were elderly and weather-beaten people , their faces intricately creased with lines and wrinkles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She was fun , easy to love , &amp;rdquo ; says Karen Wallace , who met Richards when she was in middle school . Richards became a friend to her daughter , Margot . &amp;ldquo ; We immediately loved her . She was open , fun-loving , a kind person . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first hints of mental illness appeared in Richards&amp;rsquo ; teen years . She became increasingly withdrawn , and seemed to prefer spending time in her room . She spent two years at Algonquin College and friends believe the self-harm got worse then . She started wearing long sleeves all the time to cover her scars. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the past decade , Richards has been treated in almost every hospital in Ottawa . Around 2011 , she started seeing her family doctor about low moods and bulimia. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was the hard part for any parent . One day , she would seem happy . If you saw her , you wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have got the idea that she was a depressed person , &amp;rdquo ; says her mother , Jennifer Richards . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After she graduated in 2012 , Richards embarked on a degree in social work at Carleton University . She overdosed on Tylenol in December 2013 and was admitted to the General campus of The Ottawa Hospital . She later told her friends there had been previous suicide attempts , but she had kept them private . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2014 , Richards began to see a psychiatrist and took part in an outpatient program for eating disorders . She continued to struggle with depression , but found the structure of university helpful and graduated from Carleton with distinction in 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In May 2016 , Richards overdosed and was admitted to intensive care at the Montfort Hospital . She was discharged two days afterwards without a referral for mental health support , say her friends . A couple of days later , she admitted herself to the General campus because she was still experiencing suicidal thoughts . She stayed there for about three weeks then transferred to The Royal and was released around June 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the fall of 2016 , Richards had an overdose and spent months at The Royal . She had another episode in November 2016 , which sent her to the ICU at the General campus for about a week and was then sent back to the Royal . She started electroconvulsive therapy while at The Royal , which affected her memory . She was prescribed medications and took lithium injections. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;ALSO : &lt;a href= " https : **33;34514;TOOLONG ... policies in Canada and beyond : What 's working and what needs to change&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brooke Clark met Richards while they were both at The Royal . Being admitted to hospital was like winning a lottery , says Clark . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People don&amp;rsquo ; t want to deal with it . Psychiatrists don&amp;rsquo ; t want to deal with it . It&amp;rsquo ; s like a revolving door . Being rejected is like a slap in the face , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; We talked about the revolving door . We talked about the fact that they would stop admitting borderline people after a while . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards was discharged from The Royal in February 2017 . Dialectic behaviour therapy &amp;mdash ; known as DBT , the psychotherapy that those in the mental health field have called the &amp;ldquo ; gold standard&amp;rdquo ; for treating BPD &amp;mdash ; was what she needed to get better . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The waiting list for DBT was months long . She began the program in September 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DBT takes about a year . It uses mindfulness training &amp;mdash ; how to accept and live in the moment &amp;mdash ; and teaches patients how to cope in a crisis , manage distressing emotions without trying to escape , ask for what they need and say no when they must . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One of the things to do is to accept the unacceptable . This is what you have to do , &amp;rdquo ; says Leenaars. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DBT seemed to be the glue that was holding things together . &amp;ldquo ; She was able to pull all of those skills out of the toolbox , &amp;rdquo ; says mother Jennifer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I felt she was almost there . I was hoping it was true . You think your daughter is getting better . Then the disease just takes over . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards struggled with the fear of knowing that the DBT program would end . She felt she had no stability and feared abandonment . Two weeks before the program ended , she stopped showing up because she was upset it was ending . To try to ease her transition , the DBT psychiatrist said Richards could choose between herself and the community psychiatrist . This decision was too difficult for her to make , said her friends . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34549;TOOLONG ... title= " Lauren Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019 . Just weeks before , she had posted on Instagram about mental health . " alt= " Lauren Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019 . Just weeks before , she had posted on Instagram about mental health . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;34585;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382525 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards&amp;rsquo ; friends and family believe there must be something that could have made a difference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to start thinking outside of the box . We could all have used a course , &amp;rdquo ; says Karen Wallace . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If someone has cancer , we don&amp;rsquo ; t turn them away from the door of the hospital . There&amp;rsquo ; s something out there . We have to figure out how to tap into it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jennifer says there was not enough professional support for Richards and the people who were close to her . Jennifer found dealing with her daughter&amp;rsquo ; s mental illness to be isolating and at times overwhelming . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I believe that our health-care system must place more emphasis on suicide prevention , to give more support and direction not only to those individuals who are suffering , but also to their loved ones . I was not made aware of any support group for loved ones , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Despite visiting Lauren daily for long periods of time while she was in the hospital , we received little information and guidance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Health professionals could do more to educate and support the families of patients and include them in safety or action plans that could result in greatly improved outcomes for patients at risk , she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Obviously , patient confidentiality is important , but common sense and judgement don&amp;rsquo ; t seem to factor in as they should in the case of suicidal individuals . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There would be no question that a patient whose kidneys have failed needs dialysis , but the same is not true of patients with mental health conditions , says Leenaars . Help is not available , or there&amp;rsquo ; s a long waiting list . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Suicide is a public health epidemic . But very little is being done . Hospitals in the 90s had more services available than there are now . It&amp;rsquo ; s all bare bones now , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t have a solution , &amp;rdquo ; says Margot Wallace . &amp;ldquo ; But if these populations are so vulnerable , why do they have to advocate for themselves ? I think there has to be a better way to do this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34615;TOOLONG ... title= " Rachelle Bourget ( R ) and Margot Wallace were friends of Lauren Richards , who died by suicide on February 21 . April 10 , 2019. " alt= " Rachelle Bourget ( R ) and Margot Wallace were friends of Lauren Richards , who died by suicide on February 21 . April 10 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;34651;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382523 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is the sad truth that between five and 10 per cent of people with BPD die by suicide , says Dr. Joel Paris , a psychiatrist , expert in BPD and professor emeritus at McGill University , where he was chair of the department of psychiatry between 1997 and 2007. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The scientific question is whether we know enough to predict and prevent such events . Unfortunately we don&amp;rsquo ; t . And that is why I have suggested working with suicidal patients in outpatient therapy rather than hospitalizing them . Most experts on BPD agree with me on this point . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guilt is one of the most common reactions of families and friends to a suicide . That and wondering what else they could have done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is understandable , but fails to take into account that some patients have such a strong wish to die that they come to believe this is the only way to relieve their pain , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; And we don&amp;rsquo ; t know whether we could reduce the suicide rate with a better mental health system . Suicide prevention remains a hope rather than a proven thing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s tricky to draw firm conclusions about whether there has been any success in preventing suicide among BPD patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One can not compare different studies to show that we are succeeding in preventing suicide in BPD . I would like to believe that this is the case , given all the effort we have put into helping clinicians and their families identify this disorder , &amp;rdquo ; says Paris . &amp;ldquo ; But I see no way to prove it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Youth suicide in Canada has gone down in recent decades , for example , says Paris . &amp;ldquo ; But we really have no idea why . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Organizing safety networks of supportive friends and family probably does good . But Paris , again , has seen no research showing they make a difference in death by suicide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We do not have good data on how many BPD patients in Canada die by suicide . Again , you can not generalize from following a clinical sample , and we have no population data , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been studies in the past in Montreal using psychological autopsy &amp;mdash ; in-depth family interviews after death &amp;mdash ; which suggest that about 30 per cent of youth suicides have BPD. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But the majority of these cases were men . Most of our patients are women , and it is well known that men are more likely to die by suicide , while women are more likely to make multiple attempts , &amp;rdquo ; Paris says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ******** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards belonged to a close-knit circle of friends and they refused to give up on her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Giving up on Lauren wasn&amp;rsquo ; t an option , ever , &amp;rdquo ; says her friend Rachelle Bourget. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Her friends created a safety net and were in constant contact . They smuggled her family&amp;rsquo ; s dog Buster into The Royal in a backpack during one long-term stay . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Lauren was a charmer . We would go up to her room at The Royal and the nurse would be braiding her hair , &amp;rdquo ; says Bourget. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards didn&amp;rsquo ; t have the typical profile of a BPD patient . She didn&amp;rsquo ; t abuse drugs or alcohol or become embroiled in turbulent , high-drama relationships . She had ambition , embarking on an online master&amp;rsquo ; s degree while holding down two jobs . She adopted a rescue greyhound , who she named Winston . It seemed to be a signal that she wanted something to tether herself to life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She fought for the life she wanted , &amp;rdquo ; says Bourget. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Much of what Richards&amp;rsquo ; friends and family know about the trajectory of her illness they have had to piece together through what she was willing to reveal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This whole idea that we have created a ( society ) where there is no stigma is not true , &amp;rdquo ; says Karen Wallace . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2018 , Richards started to research &amp;ldquo ; more lethal&amp;rdquo ; ways to die . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She called the psychiatrist who she saw on a weekly basis while in the DBT program to say that she wanted to kill herself , &amp;rdquo ; says mother Jennifer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The DBT psychiatrist stayed on the phone with Richards while also calling for the police to pick her up . &amp;ldquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t think this incident resulted in a hospital admission ; we believe that she was brought to see psychiatry and later that night discharged directly from the emergency room . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to her friends , there were many more times Richards either brought herself to the emergency room or called police to come get her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34681;TOOLONG ... title= " Lauren Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019 . She was 26. " alt= " Lauren Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019 . She was 26. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;34717;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382526 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In December , Richards self-harmed to the point of needing stitches . No follow up with mental health was ordered . She was sent home , says Jennifer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards was working in a coffee shop , and early this year , she started a second job . She was also taking an online graduate diploma program in mental health and addictions through Dalhousie University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When it came to her own mental health , she was sometimes desperate . In mid-January , she spent a few nights in the emergency psychiatric ward because she was feeling suicidal , but was not admitted , says Jennifer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She would leave to go to work for the next day , then come back . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Richards cut herself on Jan. 22 and was admitted to surgery , where she received stitches . According to her friends , she did not see a social worker or mental health worker . She was admitted to the hospital again on Jan. 30 . She was sent home and , as far as her friends knew , had not been counselled to change anything she was doing . Her friends could see that she was not doing well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She was also assigned a new mental health worker on Feb. 13 . Mental health workers changed often and Richards came to think of this as another revolving door . She relied on the emergency department because community supports were not enough , say her friends . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She had to re-tell her story every time , &amp;rdquo ; says Bourget. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ******** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Suicide is not inevitable for people with BPD . Some move on to live happy lives . Sometimes , on the other side , they&amp;rsquo ; re incredulous of how intensely they had once wanted to die . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Joel Paris said he doesn&amp;rsquo ; t know how to predict who will change , or when , but these changes are common . Patients get better for several reasons . People are all a little less impulsive after 30 , possibly because of brain maturation , he said . People learn to avoid situations that are difficult for them . And patients who benefit from therapy learn to apply more effective skills . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our group in Montreal has shown that the vast majority of patients we treated over the past 15 years went on the recovery , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; BPD is a disorder that usually gets better with time and responds positively to well-designed psychotherapy. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a bombshell 2011 interview , Dr. Marsha Linehan , the pioneering psychologist who developed dialectical behaviour therapy , revealed that she herself had borderline personality disorder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Linehan was hospitalized in 1961 , at the age of 17 . She had burned her wrists with cigarettes , slashed her arms , legs and midsection with sharp objects , she told The New York Times . When there were none of these in her locked seclusion room , she banged her head against the wall and the floor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I felt totally empty , like the Tin Man ; I had no way to communicate what was going on , no way to understand it , &amp;rdquo ; she told the Times . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Linehan was a devout Catholic . Things changed for her after she had what she described as a &amp;ldquo ; shimmering&amp;rdquo ; religious vision . She felt she loved herself . A year later , after a relationship ended , she still felt devastated , but didn&amp;rsquo ; t feel she had to harm herself . Linehan went on to get a PhD in 1971 and set about trying to understand what had changed . She called the concept &amp;ldquo ; radical acceptance&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; accepting yourself as you are . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;34747;TOOLONG ... title= " Lauren Richards with her rescue greyhound , Winston . Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019. " alt= " Lauren Richards with her rescue greyhound , Winston . Richards took her own life on Feb. 21 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;34783;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1382527 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kate , a 38-year-old Ottawa woman , also came out the other side . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kate , who asked that her last name not be used , had anorexia and tried to take her life through overdoses on multiple occasions between her teens and early 30s . She was eventually diagnosed with BPD and Bipolar II , which has less elevated &amp;ldquo ; highs&amp;rdquo ; than bipolar disorder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She wanted to die , and her family did not doubt it . &amp;ldquo ; It feels like you&amp;rsquo ; re drowning with no help . My parents were resigned to the fact that I would die . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; During one of Kate&amp;rsquo ; s hospital stays , she recalls a doctor telling her mother : &amp;ldquo ; How can you stand having her as a daughter ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That stung . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; People who feel mental pain are not treated like those who have physical health problems , she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; How would you treat someone with cancer or a broken limb ? Would you just send them home ? This was very serious . It was a life-or-death experience . I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t looking for attention . I really wanted to die . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When Kate was 32 , things changed all at once . She can&amp;rsquo ; t say for certain what prompted the change , but credits a supportive doctor , being treated with hormones and antidepressants as well as the DBT strategies she had learned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My doctor didn&amp;rsquo ; t shame me or embarrass me . She tried to understand . She said the way I felt things was beautiful . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps it was coincidence , but it was like turning on a light , says Kate . &amp;ldquo ; My depression literally stopped . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She still feels anxiety and uses the strategies she learned in DBT . Now , it&amp;rsquo ; s scary for her to think she was once so determined to die . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s nothing I want more than to live . Part of my recovery is to be thankful for the small things &amp;mdash ; the flowers outside , my cat on my lap , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When you have gone through something that dark , you evolve . I know so much more about myself . I know exactly what I want and what I don&amp;rsquo ; t . It&amp;rsquo ; s very peaceful . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;If you or someone you know is in distress , call one of these help lines : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul&gt; &lt;li&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //crisisline.ca/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Ottawa Mental Health Crisis Line&lt;/a&gt; : 613-722-6914 , 1-866-996-0991 if outside Ottawa&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt; &lt;a href= " https : //www.dcottawa.on.ca/ " title= " Open new window to view https : //www.dcottawa.on.ca/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Distress Centre of Ottawa and Region&lt;/a&gt; : 613-238-3311&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;34813;TOOLONG ... title= " Open new window to view https : **39;34848;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Youth Services Bureau 24/7 Crisis Line&lt;/a&gt; : 613-260-2360 or 1-877-377-7775 ( Eastern Ontario ) &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt; &lt;a href= " https : //kidshelpphone.ca/ " title= " Open new window to view https : //kidshelpphone.ca/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Kids Help Phone&lt;/a&gt; : **25;34889;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //centredaide247.com/ " title= " Open new window to view http : //centredaide247.com/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Centre d'aide 24/7&lt;/a&gt; ( French ) : **25;34916;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //telaideoutaouais.ca/ " title= " Open new window to view http : //telaideoutaouais.ca/ " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Tel-aide Outaouais&lt;/a&gt; ( French ) : 613-741-6433&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Source : Ottawa Public Health&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Joanne LauciusThu , 18 Apr 2019 12:29:50 **45;34943;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLife StoryLocal NewsMenWomenEgan : Five died of drug overdose in four days . What now ? http : **36;34990;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;35028;TOOLONG ... title= " The cluster of overdoses that struck Ottawa in late March appears to involve mixing a fentanyl-type drug into " regular " cocaine . " alt= " The cluster of overdoses that struck Ottawa in late March appears to involve mixing a fentanyl-type drug into " regular " cocaine . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;35064;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 440329 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; If five pedestrians died at a busy crosswalk in a 72-hour period , or five people died in a week from toxic lettuce , the clocks would stop , as the poets say , the authorities would cluster , everything menial set aside . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;But five sons or fathers or mothers who die from drug overdoses in three or four days are , pretty much , a statistic , a sad tally of self-infliction . Maybe five more will die next week , for all we know , for all we 're doing differently today . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( And , indeed , it was in London , Ont. &amp;mdash ; not Ottawa &amp;mdash ; where five more men have just died in a six-day period , all of suspected overdoses , prompting the police chief to make a public appeal. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The cluster of overdoses that struck Ottawa in late March has frighteningly occurred across North America . That it appears to involve mixing a fentanyl-type drug into &amp;ldquo ; regular&amp;rdquo ; cocaine is the most alarming aspect , as it exposes a whole population of occasional users to deadly possibilities . The demise of a promising musician and a middle-aged HVAC operator attest to the scary , new territory . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The experts tell us these are users who would not normally have on hand naloxone , the opioid overdose antidote . And the reason is simple : typically , fentanyl , the synthetic opioid that can slow elephants , is added to boost other opioids , like heroin or other morphine-based drugs . Cocaine , meanwhile , is not an opioid , but is being targeted just the same . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " It is our belief that most of the illicit drug market right now is tainted with fentanyl , " says Andrew Hendriks , Ottawa Public Health 's director of health protection and a leader on an overdose prevention task force . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35094;TOOLONG ... title= " Andrew Hendriks , director of health protection at Ottawa Public Health . " alt= " Andrew Hendriks , director of health protection at Ottawa Public Health . " data-portal-copyright= " Jean Levac " **28;35130;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1372132 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is unclear at this point exactly which drugs killed the five individuals in late March , but history is a tour guide to the most likely scenario in which dozens of local residents overdosed in a matter of hours &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; complete mayhem&amp;rdquo ; in the words of an experienced street worker downtown . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the summer of 2016 , in a four-day period in July , there was a dramatic increase in the number of overdose patients who arrived in the emergency department at Surrey Memorial Hospital outside Vancouver &amp;mdash ; 43 in all , mostly men . Because of the rapid use of naloxone , 35 patients were treated and soon released , but three ended up in intensive care . One died . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Due to the extraordinary nature of the event , health authorities took the time to do the toxicology . The conclusion was that there was likely a single dealer responsible for distributing crack cocaine spiked with furanyl-fentanyl , a so-called &amp;ldquo ; analog&amp;rdquo ; not seen in Surrey before . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is the first reported cluster of overdose events caused by crack cocaine contaminated with furanyl-fentanyl in North American , &amp;rdquo ; says a report submitted to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It would not be the last . Late one night in September of that same year , nine young people in Delta , B.C. , scored some cocaine . In the course of roughly 20 minutes , all nine of them overdosed and needed paramedic or hospital attention for respiratory distress . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Why ? The cocaine was laced with fentanyl or its cousins and the casual users were not habituated to opiate use , which sounds eerily like Ottawa in March 2019 . ( Briefly put , cocaine is an &amp;ldquo ; up&amp;rdquo ; and opioids are a &amp;ldquo ; down , &amp;rdquo ; to the point the respiratory system can stop. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;In June 2018 , about 20 people in Philadelphia -- regular cocaine users from a corner of the city -- were taken to hospital with overdoses in what one physician described as the first coke-fentanyl wave to strike the city . Two of the users died . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Ottawa police have declined to discuss what might have been at play last week &amp;mdash ; was it a single source , for instance ? &amp;mdash ; we do know something about the illicit drugs being consumed in the city . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The supervised injection site at the Sandy Hill Community Health Centre has used a mass spectrometer to test drugs for up to 18 substances before they are used . The process is voluntary and only takes a couple of minutes . In December , of six cocaine samples , one contained fentanyl , two others had another opioid . In November , of eight samples , four had fentanyl or similar drugs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s amazing how quickly the drug supply proliferated with all these different kinds of cocktails , &amp;rdquo ; said program director Rob Boyd , with fentanyl showing up in a sizable portion of the &amp;ldquo ; stimulant stream . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35160;TOOLONG ... title= " Rob Boyd , the head of the Oasis harm-reduction program at the Sandy Hill Community Health Centre . " alt= " Rob Boyd , the head of the Oasis harm-reduction program at the Sandy Hill Community Health Centre . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;35196;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1372134 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next step is to gather data on the amount of each drug in the mixture , he added , urging safe practices among those using cocaine , even occasionally . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Dan Ciccarone is a physician and researcher at the University of California in San Francisco . He&amp;rsquo ; s looked at many overdose waves that have struck across North America and spoken to many drug dealers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These clusters make everyone in the public health world scratch their heads , &amp;rdquo ; he said Wednesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His best explanation is that accidental contamination is the likely culprit &amp;mdash ; in shipping and packaging &amp;mdash ; not purposeful mixing of an &amp;ldquo ; up&amp;rdquo ; drug ( cocaine ) , with a &amp;ldquo ; down&amp;rdquo ; ( synthetic opioid ) , even though fentanyl is relatively cheap and easy to ship and mix . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; On the first level , you have to ask why ( mix ) . I have to tell you there&amp;rsquo ; s no good answer . &amp;rdquo ; Fentanyl and its analogs are showing up in just about everything , including meth , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; All drug streams now all of a sudden have a new level of paranoia in them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When asked about the stigma of drug use , Ciccarone said the mysterious death of race horses in California is getting more media coverage than the &amp;ldquo ; historic&amp;rdquo ; opioid crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is so big in the United States that it&amp;rsquo ; s driving the mortality rate up . And yet it&amp;rsquo ; s not being hailed at that level of crisis . I think that&amp;rsquo ; s because of stigma and fear , &amp;rdquo ; he said , then pointing to the slow public health response to the AIDS crisis of the 1980s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s taking a long time to develop a response to fentanyl because we&amp;rsquo ; re afraid of it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;BY THE NUMBERS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 56&amp;nbsp ; Times so-called &amp;lsquo ; purple&amp;rsquo ; samples ( sold as heroin , &amp;lsquo ; down&amp;rsquo ; , or fentanyl ) contained fentanyl and its derivatives from Sept to Dec , at the Sandy Hill safe injection site . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 50&amp;nbsp ; Percentage of cocaine samples that contained a synthetic opioid in November , December , in limited Sandy Hill tests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 14&amp;nbsp ; Hours between six overdoses attended by paramedics on the same day , including three fatalities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 40 , 5 : &amp;nbsp ; Emergency departments visits , deaths , from opioid-related causes in June 2018 , Ottawa area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; $80,000/$1.6 million : &amp;nbsp ; Cost of one kilogram of cut fentanyl , eventual profit from same kilogram , according to the New York Times . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; $0 or $1 : Cost of an ( antidote ) naloxone kit at many pharmacies and of limited-use fentanyl test strip . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To contact Kelly Egan , please call 613-726-5896 or email &lt;a href= " **26;35226;TOOLONG " **33;35254;TOOLONG . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;35289;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;35318;TOOLONG ... Something to consider after a concussionhttp : **30;35470;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;35502;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " Alessio Lin " **28;35538;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377421 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even casual fans have heard or read references to players being in &amp;ldquo ; concussion protocol&amp;rdquo ; for one sports league or another . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That usually means the athlete is sidelined for a week or more while progressing through mandatory stages of recovery such as a rest period , cognitive tests , increasing levels of physical activity and contact and medical or training staff clearance before returning to competition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Return to work&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; return to classroom&amp;rdquo ; protocols exist , too , but w &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;hat about the act of getting to the arena , gym , office or school ? &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s not much more than common sense involved with &amp;ldquo ; return to driving , &amp;rdquo ; so this represents an opportune time to reference a University of Georgia finding of a negative impact on driving-simulator performances even 48 hours after concussed individuals no longer experience symptoms and medical professionals deem them clinically recovered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These &amp;ldquo ; drivers&amp;rdquo ; are less able to centre virtual vehicles in the lane and allowed the vehicles to hit road shoulders more frequently , especially when negotiating curves , the 2017 study found . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A followup study of student-athletes with reported concussions says most of them will not stop driving at any point , even though they believe it&amp;rsquo ; s unsafe to drive immediately following a concussion . More than one in three say driving restrictions will &amp;ldquo ; definitely&amp;rdquo ; or &amp;ldquo ; probably&amp;rdquo ; influence decisions to report brain injuries to health-care providers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35568;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Julianne Schmidt is an assistant professor in the Department of Kinesiology at the University of Georgia in Athens , Ga . " data-portal-copyright= " University of Georgia " **28;35604;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377383 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It would be really easy to say , &amp;lsquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s simple . Let&amp;rsquo ; s just say anybody with a concussion can not drive until their symptoms go away , ' &amp;rdquo ; says Julianne Schmidt , lead author for both studies and an assistant professor in the university&amp;rsquo ; s kinesiology department . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But we have to think about the negative consequences that could actually deter people from seeking care to begin with , which is already a surmountable problem that we face in sports medicine . Just to get them to come in the door at all is a challenge , so we have to think about the negative consequences of what extensive driving restrictions mean . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario Ministry of Transportation provisions revised in 2018 require physicians , nurse practitioners and optometrists to report &amp;ldquo ; certain high-risk medical conditions , functional impairments and visual conditions , &amp;rdquo ; including cognitive impairment , sudden incapacitation , motor or sensory impairment , visual impairment , substance-use disorders and psychiatric illness . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35634;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Julianne Schmidt stands as graduate student Michelle Weber demonstrates use of a driving simulator as part of a study led by Dr. Schmidt into the effects of concussions on an individual 's ability to drive a motor vehicle .
