" data-portal-copyright= " Cassie Wright " **28;35670;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377385 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In assessing fitness to drive , a spokesman adds , the ministry relies on those regulations and on Canadian Council of Motor Transport Administrators national standards that list concussion as a &amp;ldquo ; transient&amp;rdquo ; or temporary impairment with no likelihood of recurring episodic or ongoing persistent impairment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Depending on the severity of the concussion , however , some practitioners may choose to report the condition if they are of the opinion that the person poses a risk while driving , &amp;rdquo ; the ministry&amp;rsquo ; s statement adds . &amp;ldquo ; Where significant cognitive or motor/sensory impairment is noted , a licence suspension may be issued . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Jamie Dow of the Soci&amp;eacute ; t&amp;eacute ; de l&amp;rsquo ; assurance automobile du Qu&amp;eacute ; bec says someone who has a concussion should probably not drive for at least another day or two , when they no longer have headaches and normal neck movement returns , but the provincial auto insurance agency isn&amp;rsquo ; t concerned about a temporary condition that should resolve itself in 48-72 hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35700;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Jamie Dow . " alt= " Dr. Jamie Dow . " data-portal-copyright= " PROVIDED " **28;35736;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377390 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Quebec&amp;rsquo ; s highway code also requires all drivers to demonstrate that , despite any incapacity , they can still drive correctly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The problem for many people is nothing important happens when they&amp;rsquo ; re driving , so they&amp;rsquo ; re on autopilot or they&amp;rsquo ; re thinking about 101 other things , whatever it is . They&amp;rsquo ; re listening to the radio , singing along &amp;hellip ; , &amp;rdquo ; says Dow , SAAQ&amp;rsquo ; s medical spokesman . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But , when you are not ill or you don&amp;rsquo ; t have a problem , if something happens , you can stop whatever you&amp;rsquo ; re doing and you have the reserves to be able to cope with any emergency , we hope . &amp;rdquo ; However , distracted driving is still a major cause of accidents or crashes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But I think it&amp;rsquo ; s common sense that , if you&amp;rsquo ; re not feeling well , then you&amp;rsquo ; re probably better off not driving , just in case something happens that you have to respond to rapidly . After a concussion , you&amp;rsquo ; re not thinking normally . You&amp;rsquo ; re slower , mainly , and , when you&amp;rsquo ; re on the road , you don&amp;rsquo ; t have more time to handle an emergency because you&amp;rsquo ; re not feeling well . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35766;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Shawn Marshall of The Ottawa Hospital often works with patients who need treatment for concussions. " alt= " Dr. Shawn Marshall of The Ottawa Hospital often works with patients who need treatment for concussions. " data-portal-copyright= " David Kawai " **28;35802;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377389 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;35832;TOOLONG at 100 km/h&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Shawn Marshall says patients frequently ask him if they can drive after a concussion or other more serious brain injury . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The answer is usually &amp;ldquo ; yes , &amp;rdquo ; says Marshall , the division head for physical medicine and rehabilitation at the Ottawa Hospital , but he adds that he reminds every driver they&amp;rsquo ; re responsible for being fit for driving . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Marshall , who has research affiliations with University of Ottawa Brain and Mind Research Institute , Ottawa Hospital and Bruy&amp;egrave ; re Research Institute , compares concussion impairments to those for alcohol or marijuana : Having a drink or smoking a joint is legal , but an individual is temporarily impaired from driving because they&amp;rsquo ; ve decided to do that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Immediately after an acute concussion , an individual is not fit to drive , Marshall says , and a &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;nyone with a concussion is cognitively affected in three areas , which are all relevant to driving : &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;* Information-processing speed , or how quickly they take in information and use it ; &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;* Dividing their attention between tasks like checking the speedometer , then looking down the road and refocusing on the rear-view mirror ; &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Ability to focus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Guess what&amp;rsquo ; s really important for driving ? &amp;rdquo ; Marshall says . &amp;ldquo ; Well , the ability to multi-task and the ability to take in information quickly . You&amp;rsquo ; re moving at 100 km/h . Our bodies weren&amp;rsquo ; t made to move at 100 km/h . Information is coming to you , your focal point is changing at 100 km/h . We fully don&amp;rsquo ; t understand how much brain power it&amp;rsquo ; s using . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35861;TOOLONG ... title= " This is a screen shot from the simulator used in a study about concussions and the impact they can have on an individual 's ability to drive a motor vehicle in a study led by Dr. Julianne Schmidt , an assistant professor in the Department of Kinesiology at the University of Georgia in Athens , Ga . " data-portal-copyright= " PROVIDED " **28;35897;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377384 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Many possible signs&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Charles Tator , director of the Canadian Concussion Centre in Toronto , told a hearing of the House of Commons Subcommittee on Sports-Related Concussions in February that Canadians reported 200,000 concussions annually . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And most people with concussions don&amp;rsquo ; t show up at physician&amp;rsquo ; s offices or emergency rooms , says Blaine Hoshizaki , director of the University of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Neurotrauma Impact Science Lab . Even if they know they&amp;rsquo ; ve taken hits to the head and experience headaches or confusion , they often don&amp;rsquo ; t link those factors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35927;TOOLONG ... title= " Blaine Hoshizaki is director of the University of Ottawa 's Neurotrauma Impact Science Lab. " alt= " Blaine Hoshizaki is director of the University of Ottawa 's Neurotrauma Impact Science Lab. " data-portal-copyright= " Colin Rowe Photography " **28;35963;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377451 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Because the signs or symptoms are so inconsistent , if they got hit in the head , they have to be aware of all the signs , any sorts of inconsistency with vision , balance , &amp;rdquo ; Hoshizaki says . &amp;ldquo ; I think there&amp;rsquo ; s probably close to 60 symptoms , so , if you hit your head and you recognize any of these , then you should be concerned because the problem we have is that driving is a pretty complex thing that we kind of learn to do almost automatically . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So , how that influences awareness alone , knowing that there&amp;rsquo ; s a person in your blind spot , that&amp;rsquo ; s a pretty subtle sort of cognitive responsibility , that you&amp;rsquo ; re driving along and you&amp;rsquo ; ve seen a guy and you know he&amp;rsquo ; s driven up into your blind spot . If you&amp;rsquo ; re cognitively compromised , that little thing might miss &amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are so many elements to how your brain functions that it&amp;rsquo ; s almost automatic , that you won&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily pick up when you&amp;rsquo ; re missing it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;35993;TOOLONG ... title= " A model of a brain . " alt= " A model of a brain . " data-portal-copyright= " Jesse Orrico " **28;36029;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1188061 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Individual **26;36059;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Schmidt says other conditions such as severe traumatic brain injury ( TBI ) , stroke or Parkinson&amp;rsquo ; s disease aren&amp;rsquo ; t as transient as concussion , which means they won&amp;rsquo ; t go away and are easier to identify , which in turn eases the process of getting an individual into disability services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concussions typically require 10- to 14-day restrictions , Schmidt adds , but the unknown is exactly how long concussion-related impairment lasts beyond symptoms , and what about potential differences based on age , gender or other demographic factors ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We used college-age athletes and non-athletes &amp;mdash ; mostly non-athletes &amp;mdash ; but what does this look like in your 16-year-old adolescent driver ? What does this look like in your 50-year-old driver , &amp;rdquo ; Schmidt says . &amp;ldquo ; Concussion recoveries can differ quite a bit in those groups , as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So , I don&amp;rsquo ; t have an answer for you , but I&amp;rsquo ; m curious about the same thing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **25;36087;TOOLONG in daily life&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dow says medical personnel examining a patient with a possible concussion or head injury often start out by asking that individual if they can move their fingers or hands or having them count backwards , but they don&amp;rsquo ; t think to tell that person to avoid driving for a few days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Medical personnel &amp;ldquo ; tend to forget&amp;rdquo ; that driving is an everyday activity , particularly for Canadians who don&amp;rsquo ; t live in major city centres , he adds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To counter that situation , SAAQ has a workshop for physicians and other health personnel to underline the importance of driving and how it should be considered when they&amp;rsquo ; re talking about the impact of whatever has happened on a patient&amp;rsquo ; s life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; To be fair , Hoshizaki says , evaluating incapacity after a concussion is difficult even for specialists , so imagine the dilemma of a layperson trying to figure out if he or she is ready to return behind the wheel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Physicians can provide guidance , Hoshizaki adds , but there&amp;rsquo ; s no real way of telling people they can&amp;rsquo ; t drive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When we talk about guidelines to return to play or ( other ) guidelines , they&amp;rsquo ; re guidelines , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re not rules . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dow says the process of removing a driver&amp;rsquo ; s licence takes about six weeks , so , effectively , SAAQ tells Quebec physicians to report conditions only if the consequence of previous injury or disease &amp;mdash ; formally called &amp;ldquo ; sequelae&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; last six months or more . As one example , he refers to a former municipal transit driver who , following a severe concussion , functioned at the cognitive level of an eight-year-old . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Temporary&amp;rdquo ; conditions such as concussions do not have to be reported to provincial authorities unless they affect driving and a patient says he&amp;rsquo ; ll drive anyway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The point is , if you were advised by your physician not to drive and you do drive and you kill somebody , you can be liable for accidental homicide , which is something you have to bear in mind , &amp;rdquo ; Dow says . &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re also going to have that on your conscience for the rest of your life . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Looking for **25;36114;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It would be easier to advise someone not to return behind the wheel after a concussion based on specific medical indicators or &amp;ldquo ; biomarkers , &amp;rdquo ; but nobody has figured that out , despite what Hoshizaki describes as &amp;ldquo ; a fair bit of money&amp;rdquo ; invested in research in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are known biomarkers for brain injury , Marshall adds , but , because brain injury is a spectrum , even those biomarkers are not useful enough in diagnosing concussion. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Similarly , magnetic resonance imaging ( MRI ) or diffusion tensor imaging ( DTI ) scans can reveal signs of concussion , but Marshall says that again they are neither sensitive nor specific enough to be gold-standard tests for diagnosing concussions . In other words , they could miss diagnosing concussions at the individual level , which is a clinical diagnosis anyway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The ideal , Schmidt says , would be to use current clinical tools to predict or find patient patterns differentiating between someone who shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t drive and someone who could . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Even though they both have a concussion , maybe you&amp;rsquo ; re having issues with reaction time , and we know that is a better predictor , better indicator of your driving safety , &amp;rdquo ; she says . &amp;ldquo ; However , you&amp;rsquo ; re having more issues with your memory , which might not be as related to your safety in driving . It might hurt your ability to navigate in driving , but you&amp;rsquo ; re OK , your safety on the road is fine &amp;hellip ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My second preference , then , if that&amp;rsquo ; s not possible because it&amp;rsquo ; s possible that the clinical tools we use now don&amp;rsquo ; t predict those things very well or are not sensitive enough to those things that they wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t work in that way , would be then to introduce a new tool or assessment battery that was short and succinct , but got at someone&amp;rsquo ; s readiness to return to driving so that , clinically , we could return them to driving as soon , as quickly , but as safely as possible . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;36141;TOOLONG ... title= " Dr. Shawn Marshall . " alt= " Dr. Shawn Marshall . " data-portal-copyright= " PROVIDED " **28;36177;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1377388 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Out of the dark&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guidelines to concussion management have changed , with &amp;ldquo ; return to activity as tolerated&amp;rdquo ; as a general counsel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Marshall says &amp;ldquo ; return to sport , &amp;rdquo ; is easy since concussed athletes simply must go through established protocols and show themselves to be symptom-free because they&amp;rsquo ; re otherwise at increased risk of injury . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Return to school&amp;rdquo ; is hard , he says , because it&amp;rsquo ; s a distracting environment requiring cognitive and social energy , so &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;a concussed student might have to start off by avoiding&lt;/span&gt; gym or taking only one class . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For driving , perhaps a 10-minute commute would be OK , but not a long vacation trip , says Marshall , who likens it to recovery from a sprained ankle : If you can walk without pain , proceed , but , if walking causes pain , back off temporarily before trying again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Obviously , if the person is acutely confused and doesn&amp;rsquo ; t know the date or doesn&amp;rsquo ; t know where they are , they shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be driving . Most of us would say that , &amp;rdquo ; he says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We used to say , &amp;lsquo ; Go into a dark room and wait for your symptoms to get better . &amp;rsquo ; They tell them not to do that now . Now they say , &amp;lsquo ; After the first 24 to 48 hours , return to activity as tolerated . &amp;rsquo ; And , if you return to activity as tolerated , that would mean you get to make decisions on what you can handle and can&amp;rsquo ; t handle . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **28;36207;TOOLONG " **35;36237;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : //twitter.com/HolderGord " &gt;Twit ... &lt;/p&gt; Gord Holder , PostmediaSun , 07 Apr 2019 19:30:50 **45;36274;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal **36;36321;TOOLONG alcohol sales to grocery stores sends more people to hospital : studyhttp : **35;36359;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;36396;TOOLONG ... title= " In Ottawa alone , alcohol led to 6,100 emergency department visits between 2013 and 2015 , 1,270 hospitalizations during the same time and 140 deaths ( between 2007 and 2011 ) , according to Ottawa Public Health . The former Liberal government began expanding beer and wine sales to grocery stores across the province in 2015. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Researchers also compared geographic areas that contained grocery stores selling alcohol following deregulation with those that didn&amp;rsquo ; t. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Its findings were both surprising and worrisome. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;36563;TOOLONG ... title= " Beer on shelves . " alt= " Beer on shelves . " data-portal-copyright= " ERIC THOMAS " **28;36599;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1240891 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study found that , in general , alcohol-related rates of visits to emergency departments rose by 17.8 per cent during the period of the study , significantly higher than all other emergency visits . And , significantly , researchers found that the regions with grocery stores that began selling alcohol after 2015 had a six-per-cent greater increase in emergency department visits compared with regions without grocery stores selling alcohol . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The conclusion : &amp;ldquo ; Alcohol deregulation appears to have resulted in increased alcohol-related harms in Ontario . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And visits to emergency departments because of alcohol are just the tip of the iceberg , say researchers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Deregulation has likely had negative impacts far beyond the reported emergency department visits . &amp;rdquo ; That deregulation includes more stores and also longer hours . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;a name= " pda10280212 " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;div class= " PDSPoll " id= " PDIcontainer10280212 " style= " display:inline-block ; " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div id= " PDsuperContainer " &gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;script type= " text/javascript " charset= " UTF-8 " src= " https : **33;36629;TOOLONG ... &lt;noscript&gt;&lt;a href= " https : //poll.fm/10280212 " &gt;Take Our **31;36664;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Previous research has also associated increased access to alcohol with alcohol-related health harms , but Myran said this was the first time such research had been done in Ontario . The expansion of alcohol sales beyond the highly regulated Liquor Control Board of Ontario and a few stand-alone wine stores created a &amp;ldquo ; natural experiment that can be used to better understand the relationship between alcohol availability and harms. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alcohol consumption is estimated to cause 6.6 per cent of deaths in men and 2.2 per cent of deaths in women . More than 23 million Canadians over age 15 reported using alcohol in 2017 . Of those , 21 per cent reported drinking enough to be at risk of chronic and immediate alcohol related harms. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Ottawa alone , alcohol led to 6,100 emergency department visits between 2013 and 2015 , 1,270 hospitalizations during the same time and 140 deaths ( between 2007 and 2011 ) , according to Ottawa Public Health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study suggests that placing limits on the number of alcohol outlets and their weekly hours of operation &amp;ldquo ; should be part of any alcohol strategy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;36697;TOOLONG " **34;36726;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;36762;TOOLONG ... sentenced to two days in jail after pushing her friend off a 50-foot bridge&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;36797;TOOLONG ... are the anti-vaxxers ? It 's time to end the miscarriage taboo&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneFri , 29 Mar 2019 18:14:53 **45;36867;TOOLONG &amp;amp ; FitnessFamily &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenWomenShine : It 's time to end the miscarriage taboo -- and talk about women 's bodily functionshttp : **31;36914;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;36947;TOOLONG ... title= " There are still too many taboos around pregnancy , miscarriage and women 's bodies . " alt= " There are still too many taboos around pregnancy , miscarriage and women 's bodies . " data-portal-copyright= " NataliaDeriabina " **28;36983;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1308847 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the bleeding started , I didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to believe I was losing my pregnancy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I was aware this could happen . What I didn&amp;rsquo ; t expect was the pain &amp;ndash ; a lot of pain . The heavy bleeding . The weakness , the fainting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I didn&amp;rsquo ; t know how these things went , so I ended up in the emergency . Instead of rest and a cup of tea , my pregnancy ended with the holy trifecta women know all too well : harsh lights , cold instruments , technical talk . Because the &amp;ldquo ; products of conception&amp;rdquo ; had passed , I didn&amp;rsquo ; t need to undergo a &amp;ldquo ; dilation and curettage , &amp;rdquo ; which is exactly what it sounds like : the uterus is opened up and whatever is left is scraped out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The nurse cautiously told me about a support group . Just in case . Grieving ? Me ? I said I wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t need it . I was ashamed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It turns out pregnancy loss is a common part of , well , pregnancy . Somewhere between &lt;a href= " https : **33;37013;TOOLONG ... and 25 per cent&lt;/a&gt; of pregnancies end this way , on average . It&amp;rsquo ; s actually a really tough job to sew these bits of different DNA together in a coherent and functional way . Because of this , miscarriage , well , just happens . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So , why is no one talking about it ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most women , of course , have heard that early gestation is a somewhat insecure affair . But a large part of the problem lies in the everyday erasement of women&amp;rsquo ; s lived experiences , especially their bodily ones , even more so when these have to do with fertility and motherhood . As any woman will tell you , speaking about the way your own body functions is a complicated and delicate matter that makes almost everyone ill at ease . Most women included . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When I started talking about my miscarriage , a surprising number of women confided in secretive tones that they , too , had silently suffered through pregnancy loss . Many had never told anyone but their partners . Some lost friends who weren&amp;rsquo ; t able to deal with their pain . Grief , after all , is uncomfortable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What I heard , over and over again , were stories of sorrow , but of shame and anxiety too . Many blamed themselves : Did I do something wrong ? Could I have prevented this ? And , especially : Can I actually do this pregnancy thing ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Taboos that surround women&amp;rsquo ; s bodies , miscarriage shame included , can have severe consequences . Psychologically , physically and emotionally , women shoulder burdens they have little opportunities to unload , except in hushed tones with their closest allies and , in some cases , haphazardly compassionate medical personnel . Even technology is in on the pain game : The baby gear ads that had started to pop up in my news feed continued to haunt me for months , thanks to the malgorithms&amp;rsquo ; dogged determination . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;37048;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;37090;TOOLONG ... The Karlssons ' tragedy reminds us to support each other after pregnancy **25;37125;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Things may be changing , though . In the public sphere , there are attempts to shake things up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; These past years , organizations around the globe have launched campaigns to end &lt;a href= " http : **34;37152;TOOLONG ... shaming . &lt;/a&gt; &amp;rdquo ; Monthly bleeding forces women in parts of the world into hiding ; girls are excluded from school . &amp;ldquo ; Aunt Flo&amp;rdquo ; remains a source of stigma and humiliation in numerous cultures , including ours . Signs that times are changing , a menstruation &lt;a href= " https : **33;37188;TOOLONG ... &lt;/a&gt; is coming to a phone near you , and &lt;a href= " https : **33;37223;TOOLONG ... film about monthlies&lt;/a&gt; just won an Oscar . And yes , it&amp;rsquo ; s on Netflix. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;37258;TOOLONG ... title= " Let 's talk more about women 's bodies and stop shaming basic functions such as menstruation. " alt= " Let 's talk more about women 's bodies and stop shaming basic functions such as menstruation. " data-portal-copyright= " Rich Pedroncelli " **28;37294;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1370883 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; For her recently released &lt;a href= " http : **32;37324;TOOLONG " &; ... Laura Dodsworth photographed 100 vulvas and exposed these portraits with women&amp;rsquo ; s stories about their &amp;ldquo ; private parts , &amp;rdquo ; many of them laced with unease , trauma and shame , but also acceptance and self-love . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The bold and contested #shoutyourabortion movement is striving to open the discussion about abortion and tell the stories of ordinary women who made the difficult choice to end a pregnancy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All these appeals reveal that the corporeal realities of women still remain vastly hidden , both physically and socially . Pregnancy loss is no exception . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The end of the omerta is long overdue . It&amp;rsquo ; s time for us to speak up and proclaim that #miscarriagehappens . It&amp;rsquo ; s time to end the miscarriage taboo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **29;37358;TOOLONG Shine&lt;/strong&gt; is a Humanities PhD candidate and co-lead of Femmes Expertes , an organization fighting for better representation of women 's expertise in the media . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Twitter : @shineonlaura // **26;37389;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN OPINIONS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37417;TOOLONG ... He took his own life at home in November 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Five months before he died , Parent spent 56 hours in the psychiatric emergency services ( PES ) unit at the Civic campus while waiting for a bed to open on the psychiatric ward . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ellen Parent said her son developed a deep-seated fear of the unit &amp;mdash ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37670;TOOLONG ... likened its rooms to jail cells&lt;/a&gt; &amp;mdash ; which she believes deterred him from seeking more help when his mental health deteriorated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After their son&amp;rsquo ; s suicide , Ellen and her husband , Ken , launched a determined campaign to address the situation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When I saw what it had done to him , I swore that I didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to see another person go through that again , &amp;rdquo ; said Parent . &amp;ldquo ; Ryan passed away and I thought , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not going to just let this go . It&amp;rsquo ; s not right . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Parents met with hospital managers , and together they worked out a series of compromises . &amp;nbsp ; Parent , who originally wanted to see the PES unit closed , conceded that it&amp;rsquo ; s required as a safe , calm place for patients to be assessed before receiving a mental health referral . Hospital officials , after consulting with patient and family advisory groups , also conceded they could take steps to make the rooms more inviting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hospital recently installed full-spectrum lighting to simulate outdoor light , and resurfaced the unit&amp;rsquo ; s metal toilets with a powder coating to make them less institutional . The rooms have been painted a lighter colour , and one wall in each is to have a pastel shade . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hospital has also committed to adding the Discovery Channel to Plexiglas-protected televisions in the rooms , and to installing wall murals to soften the unit&amp;rsquo ; s atmosphere . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the renovations are complete , patients will also be able to communicate directly with a nurse through a security window , rather than by intercom . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;37705;TOOLONG ... title= " This is a picture that Ryan Parent took of his room at The Ottawa Hospital 's PES until . After graduating , Parent launched a moving company called Jesus Moving . He was by then an active member of the Nepean Baptist Church . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;37771;TOOLONG ... title= " Ryan Parent " alt= " Ryan Parent " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;37807;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1161382 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt;Parent believed in a hands-on form of Christianity , and often took homeless people to dinner , prayed with them and washed their feet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;After teaching himself photography , Parent launched himself in the wedding business , and later developed a software company , Vidrack , that marketed a video recording platform . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;In addition to advocating for improvements to the PES Unit , &lt;a href= " https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=fVwZvN1 ... &lt;p&gt; Ontario needs a more centralized and integrated health-care system as it copes with growing demographic challenges , a premier&amp;rsquo ; s council charged with ending hallway medicine says in its first report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The initial report from the council headed by retired hospital CEO Dr. Reuben Devlin was released Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t contain specific recommendations , but sketches a future of health care in Ontario that likely does not include the current regionalized system of LHINs ( Local Health Integration Networks ) , that is leaner and that is centrally controlled . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;div&gt; &lt;div aria-label= " Message Contents " aria-selected= " true " &gt; &lt;div class= " rpm5 " aria-label= " Expanded Message Contents " &gt; &lt;div class= " rpY4 **28;38209;TOOLONG **29;38239;TOOLONG **26;38270;TOOLONG " &gt; &lt;div dir= " ltr " &gt; **28;38325;TOOLONG lang= " en-US " &gt;Here are the findings of the report &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;Hallway Health Care : A system under strain : **35;38355;TOOLONG &lt;ul&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;1,000 patients a day were waiting for a hospital bed on an stretcher or unconventional space in 2018 ; **37;38392;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt; &lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;&lt;span lang= " en-US " &gt;Visits to emergency departments increased by 11 per cent over the past six years ; **26;38431;TOOLONG &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;Patients who require admission are spending an average of 16 hours in the emergency department before a bed is available ; &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;The median wait for long-term care was 146 days in 2017/18 ; &lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;There has been a 72 per cent increase in emergency department visits and a 79 per cent increase in in-patient admissions for children and youth with mental health issues over past 11 years . &lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s current health-care system can be characterized as decentralized , large and siloed , and it can be difficult at times to know who is responsible and accountable for ensuring Ontarians have access to high-value health care , &amp;rdquo ; the report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Stronger lines of accountability would help make the health-care system more efficient and also help ensure Ontario gets a greater value for what it currently spends on health care . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;38459;TOOLONG ... title= " Stethoscopes. " alt= " Stethoscopes. " data-portal-copyright= " Khalil Hamra " **28;38495;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1299279 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report also notes that there are 21 health-related government agencies supporting the design and delivery of health care in Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Many of these agencies were created to tackle specific problems , support research , or to establish quality standards and metrics to help the system as it matured . However , these agencies are not always well aligned and there is limited strategic oversight to ensure the efficient and co-ordinated use of resources . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The council is widely expected to kill the regional LHIN system and to replace it with a super agency in charge of everything from Public Health Ontario and the Trillium Gift of Life Network to Cancer Care Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Critics , including former deputy minister of health Dr. Bob Bell , have warned that such a super agency could decrease the quality of health care in the province . A similar centralization in British Columbia worsened cancer care in the province , Bell said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ottawa area&amp;rsquo ; s Champlain LHIN gets praise from Bell and others for achievements in co-ordinating and making health care more efficient in the region , but the LHIN system has many critics who call it unnecessarily cumbersome and bureaucratic and who say it was never given the tools to make a real difference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;38525;TOOLONG ... title= " Emergency room . " alt= " Emergency room . " data-portal-copyright= " Kelly Roche " **28;38561;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1124985 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The interim report spells out many of the well-documented challenges with health care in the province , notably overcrowded hospitals , patients waiting in hospital beds because there is not more suitable care available and the strain on the long-term care system . There are currently 72 long-term care beds per 1,000 people aged 75 or older in the province , a number that is projected to decline to 29 beds per 1,000 people over 75 by 2041 &amp;mdash ; a decline of 60 per cent or the equivalent of 48,000 bed closures &amp;ldquo ; if nothing is done , &amp;rdquo ; says the report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It acknowledges that Ontario has the lowest per capital spending on health care compared to other provinces and territories , but adds : &amp;ldquo ; The system could work smarter and use this same amount of money to achieve better health outcomes . &amp;rdquo ; Canada , compared to some other countries , spends more but scores lower on some performance indicators. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The solutions to the issues outlined in the report are what worry observers and critics . Although there are bed shortages , simply adding more beds is not the solution , the report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bell has been vocal about his concerns that rapid and massive changes could degrade Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s many health care successes , including cancer care . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sudbury MPP France G&amp;eacute ; linas , the NDP health critic , fears the changes will open the door to more privatization of health care across the province , and less input from regions &amp;mdash ; especially northern Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province is likely to be divided into five health regions , including one that includes Ottawa and encompasses most of the province east of the GTA . The northern region would include everything north of the French River . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The people who are the most excited about this are the big private healthcare providers , &amp;rdquo ; said G&amp;eacute ; linas. &amp;ldquo ; They will sell a model that is glossy that further opens the door to care based on the ability to pay . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa Hospital CEO Dr. Jack Kitts is among 14 members of the Premier&amp;rsquo ; s Council on Improving Health Care and Ending Hallway Medicine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;38591;TOOLONG ... launched in support of bus crash survivor after double amputation&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;38626;TOOLONG ... who hear more than one language at home develop attention advantage : study&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;38661;TOOLONG ... Patrick Brazeau : How family -- and a stranger 's kindness -- made life worth living&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneThu , 31 Jan 2019 23:06:29 **45;38696;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenSen . Patrick Brazeau : How family -- and a stranger 's kindness -- made life worth livinghttp : **34;38743;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;38779;TOOLONG ... title= " Senator Patrick Brazeau is shown during a weekend camping trip on the Gatineau River with Ottawa Citizen reporter Gary Dimmock in 2016 . On Bell Let 's Talk Day , Brazeau urges those who need mental health , particularly Indigenous men and boys , support to seek help . " alt= " Senator Patrick Brazeau is shown during a weekend camping trip on the Gatineau River with Ottawa Citizen reporter Gary Dimmock in 2016 . On Bell Let 's Talk Day , Brazeau urges those who need mental health , particularly Indigenous men and boys , support to seek help . " data-portal-copyright= " CHRIS MIKULA " **28;38815;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1342237 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; They say everybody gets a second chance in life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I must be one of the lucky ones , because I got three . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Three years ago , I was lying on a hospital bed , unconscious , intubated and recovering from serious injuries from a second suicide attempt . It was a time in my life when I had given up on everything and everyone around me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Today , I am preparing to once again return to work at the Senate . My newborn son , River , brings a smile to my face every day . And I&amp;rsquo ; m building a new life with my soon-to-be-wife , Marie-Claire , who means the world to me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My friends and family played a crucial role in my recovery , especially my dad , Marcel , who brought me back to basics and to what really matters in life . But there was one person &amp;mdash ; a stranger &amp;mdash ; who made me realize , at my lowest point , that life is worth living . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As I sat at the end of my bed at the Hull hospital , a surgeon came to see me . I had just woken up from a medically induced coma yet I felt like I was still living a nightmare . I was in complete shock and scared . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But what happened next changed my life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After asking the standard medical questions , the surgeon lingered . He began to talk to me on a deeper , more human level . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shaking his head , he asked me what I was doing in that hospital bed . He told me I was lucky to be alive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I recall him saying , &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re better than that , &amp;rdquo ; as I lay back in the bed with tears welling up in my eyes . He went on to tell me I had so much more to contribute to the world , and to keep on fighting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He knew that I am a senator and he even mentioned my previous role as national chief of the Congress of Aboriginal Peoples , but we were still complete strangers . At the end of the conversation , I shook his hand and he suggested I seek treatment after I leave the hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I admitted myself to the psychiatric ward at the Hull hospital for an assessment . Over the following two years , I also saw two psychiatrists who helped me process the events leading up to my near-death. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I uncovered a lot about myself in those sessions . It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t always easy or pleasant to examine my own life so closely but I learned my struggles stemmed from bouts of depression , anxiety and PTSD from turbulent moments in my life that overwhelmed me . For the first time in a long time , I began to feel like I had control over my life again after years of personal and professional turmoil had eaten away at me. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I never saw that surgeon again , but I want him to know how much his words meant to me that day , and that I am OK . Sometimes , all it takes is genuine human connection , even between strangers , to help someone who is in crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One in five Canadians will suffer from a mental illness in their lifetime . I am one of those statistics but I&amp;rsquo ; m still standing today and talking openly about mental health because that&amp;rsquo ; s what saved my life . Stigma is the main reason Canadians who suffer from a mental illness don&amp;rsquo ; t seek help . That&amp;rsquo ; s why events like Bell Let&amp;rsquo ; s Talk are so important because they help break the silence surrounding mental health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It can be difficult to ask for help . I want men and boys &amp;mdash ; especially Indigenous men and boys &amp;mdash ; to know there is no shame in asking for help when you need it . It&amp;rsquo ; s a lesson I will pass on to my newborn son . Every day that I see him , hold him , hug him and kiss him gives me strength . When I gaze into his loving eyes I know that life is indeed worth living and I feel it in my heart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **27;38845;TOOLONG . Patrick Brazeau&lt;/strong&gt; represents the Repentigny division of Quebec . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Do you need mental health support ? &lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;38874;TOOLONG ... Hospital wants rapid-access mental health clinic among inquest **25;39146;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Sen. Patrick BrazeauWed , 30 Jan 2019 12:51:36 **45;39173;TOOLONG ... StoryMenWomenConcussion researcher uses high-speed x-ray to look inside pig brains , cadavers and morehttp : **36;39220;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;39258;TOOLONG ... title= " Carleton University professor Oren Petel has developed an x-ray machine capable of taking 100,000 frames per second . " alt= " Carleton University professor Oren Petel has developed an x-ray machine capable of taking 100,000 frames per second . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;39294;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340588 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;39324;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Carleton University researcher has invented a high-speed X-ray system that&amp;rsquo ; s now being used to study the effects of collisions on football and hockey helmets , pig brains , synthetic brains and cadavers. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prof. Oren Petel conducts his research from a small , lead-lined lab on the sixth floor of Carleton&amp;rsquo ; s Canal Building . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The lab is equipped with a linear impactor , a machine&amp;nbsp ; capable of delivering a head hit at 12 metres per second , along with an X-ray system that can capture images of the collision at 100,000 frames per second . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It means Petel and his research team can recreate in spectacular detail what happens to a helmet &amp;mdash ; and to a brain &amp;mdash ; during a high-speed collision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Instead of looking just at how the head is moving , we can visualize what&amp;rsquo ; s happening to a representative brain model inside the head , &amp;rdquo ; said Petel , a professor in mechanical and aerospace engineering . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The machine&amp;rsquo ; s high-resolution images are used to create a video &amp;mdash ; the process is known as cineradiography &amp;mdash ; that shows the interaction between a helmet and a head while also revealing the brain&amp;rsquo ; s physical response to an impact : how it compresses , twists and stretches . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Taken together , Petel said , the information promises to inform the work of researchers as they design better helmets for hockey and football players , soldiers and cyclists . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s next-level information that can be used to validate the computer models used in helmet design , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;39360;TOOLONG ... title= " Here , he sets up a machine that hits the " head " with great velocity from which he can measure impact and damage to the head . " alt= " Here , he sets up a machine that hits the " head " with great velocity from which he can measure impact and damage to the head . " data-portal-copyright= " Julie Oliver " **28;39396;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340589 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Petel has submitted patent applications for his test methodology and his imaging system , which is among the fastest in the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The system became operational in the summer of 2017 , and is now being deployed in a number of concussion and helmet research projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One project , being conducted in partnership with Defence Research and Development Canada , is testing what happens to a synthetic brain inside a plastic head model . Markers are implanted inside the artificial brain to measure how it deforms in a collision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another research project , being pursued with Carleton neuroscience professor Matt Holahan , studies how pig brains ( collected from an abattoir ) respond to an impact . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re trying to understand the specific injuries that the cells undergo when the brain stretches , &amp;rdquo ; explained Petel. &amp;ldquo ; We want to know how the stretch and pull response of the brain might lead to injury and , specifically , where those injuries might be occurring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want to understand the critical limits of what those cells and those structures can take before they&amp;rsquo ; re affected . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still another project seeks to validate the computer models now commonly used in head injury research . Those models take information from impact experiments and predict how the brain will respond to the forces involved . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Working with researchers from the University of Ottawa , &amp;nbsp ; Petel&amp;rsquo ; s lab is using cadavers to test the computer models , and to add information about how regions deep in the brain are affected . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The brain is a very complex system and understanding exactly what leads to injury is not a trivial matter . We&amp;rsquo ; re still developing that understanding , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Petel took an unusual path into concussion research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Born and raised in Montreal , he developed an early interest in physics and decided to study mechanical engineering . At McGill University , he joined the Shockwave Physics Group , where he immersed himself in blast research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;39426;TOOLONG ... title= " Folds in a human brain . " alt= " Folds in a human brain . " data-portal-copyright= " David Duprey " **28;39462;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1039817 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It just sounded like a lot of fun , things impacting each other , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; And it was really challenging . Your experiments typically last several microseconds , several millionths of a second , and you have to collect all your data in the time before your experiment is destroyed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As he studied the response of ballistic armour and other protective materials to an explosion , Petel became more and more interested in the dynamics of blast injuries . What was happening internally when a body was hit by a shock wave ? What made the lungs and brain so susceptible to injury ? How was the tissue deforming ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Petel felt that better answers to those questions could improve the design of protective equipment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He began to study injury biomechanics , and , at one conference , he asked a seasoned researcher why there wasn&amp;rsquo ; t more information about the reaction of internal tissue to a blast . He was told there was no way to measure what happened at such high speeds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So why isn&amp;rsquo ; t someone developing something to measure this ? &amp;rdquo ; Petel asked . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The researcher bristled : &amp;ldquo ; If you think you&amp;rsquo ; re so smart , why don&amp;rsquo ; t you do it ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A PhD student at the time , Petel took up that challenge . In December 2010 , he launched a private company to attract investment for the development of a high-speed X-ray system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But commercial investors were unwilling to take a chance on an unproven student , so Petel set his sights on becoming a professor . He figured it was the only way to pursue his idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After an exhaustive search , he secured a job in Carleton University&amp;rsquo ; s Department of Mechanical and Aerospace Engineering in August 2013 , and applied for funding to develop his X-ray system and an impact research lab. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canada Foundation for Innovation and the Ontario Research Fund financed most of the project , which cost about $310,000 and took more than two years to complete . The Canadian Institutes of Health Research and the Natural Sciences and Engineering Research Council have contributed to the lab&amp;rsquo ; s ongoing research . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concussion research has come a long way during the past decade , Petel said , but more needs to be done to understand what happens inside the brain during a high-speed collision and how to design a helmet to better protect against injury . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He hopes his research will ultimately make it safer for athletes to compete in sports such as hockey and football . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The ultimate goal of this research is to change the way helmets are designed to improve helmet response , and also to influence in the longer term how helmets are evaluated and the safety standards applied to them . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;39492;TOOLONG " **34;39521;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;39557;TOOLONG ... border agent developed appetite for child porn after exposure at work&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;39592;TOOLONG ... remembrance cast against Hitler plan to purge Canadian Jews&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;39627;TOOLONG ... researchers warn that lack of sleep is a public health crisis&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Andrew DuffyMon , 28 Jan 2019 14:31:26 **45;39662;TOOLONG ... **27;39709;TOOLONG up support : Pain doctor fears treatment options could be restrictedhttp : **30;39738;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;39770;TOOLONG ... title= " Derek Debeer , former drummer with the band Johnny Clegg and Suvuka , who has received treatment from Dr. Ellen Thompson for back and arm pain , which plagues many professional drummers. " alt= " Derek Debeer , former drummer with the band Johnny Clegg and Suvuka , who has received treatment from Dr. Ellen Thompson for back and arm pain , which plagues many professional drummers. " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;39806;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340270 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like many professional drummers , Derek De Beer paid a price for the job he loved . There was a time when the former drummer with the South African band Savuka lived with excruciating back and elbow pain that threatened to end his drumming days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; De Beer , who has lived in Ottawa for 30 years , searched in vain for help before meeting pain specialist Dr. Ellen Thompson . She injected small amounts of local anaesthetic agents and cortisone into trigger points in the muscles around his painful joints . Attacks every year or two were treated with consistent pain relief . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The treatment worked . It allowed&amp;nbsp ; De Beer to get on with his life and work , which includes teaching drumming and using drumming in his corporate team-building business . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I play drums . I teach a lot and carry a lot . I am pretty hard on my body and it takes a toll , &amp;rdquo ; he says . Still , after having a number of treatments , De Beer has not needed the treatment for five years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said she has seen good results from the treatment in patients in severe pain and research backs up its effectiveness . But she fears that changes coming in Ontario could limit the ability of doctors to offer the inexpensive and effective treatment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;39836;TOOLONG ... title= " Derek Debeer " alt= " Derek Debeer " data-portal-copyright= " Errol McGihon " **28;39872;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1340271 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; At a time when steps are being taken to limit opioid prescribing for patients with chronic pain , the injections &amp;ldquo ; represent a major , although limited , period of relief from pain , &amp;rdquo ; Thompson said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Most chronic pain patients state that the degree of relief from local anaesthetic injections is far more profound than the relief provided by even powerful opioids. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said she is worried that such injections will be restricted after concerns were raised by the Ministry of Health about the increased use of nerve blocks and other similar injection procedures in the province between 2009 and 2015 . It has been the subject of discussions between the Ontario Medical Association and the province in ongoing negotiations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are reasons why the government may wish to put restrictions on frequency of the injections , Thompson said . Some practitioners are performing a large number of injections on patients per visit . &amp;ldquo ; Having watched some of them in action , it is clear that accuracy and precision is sacrificed to speed and a larger number of injections being performed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said some practitioners have been billing OHIP hundreds of thousands of dollar a year through performing dozens of injections per visit . There is a fee for each nerve that is injected . The fees for trigger points are less than $20 , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is a need for more sensible methods of paying pain physicians , said Thompson . &amp;ldquo ; But this must be achieved without further jeopardizing pain patients&amp;rsquo ; ability to function . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson has previously raised concerns that it has become more difficult to effectively treat chronic pain because of crackdowns on prescribing opioids . The growth in injections could reflect physicians searching for chronic pain relief for their patients without relying on opioids. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twenty per cent of the population suffers from chronic pain , she noted and treatments that allow sufferers to work , sleep and function are crucial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thompson said long-lasting chronic pain can often be prevented if the injections are administered in the first few weeks after the onset of pain . For patients with ongoing chronic pain , the treatment can allow them to get some relief to sleep , work and live their lives , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Back pain is one of the most expensive problems in all first world countries . No one is treating patients optimally to prevent chronic pain anywhere . We have the skill and knowledge . We need to start using it , even if it will remove a good source of steady income for a large number of clinics and their private investors . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Recent studies have questioned costly spinal fusion surgery that is sometimes offered as a last resort option for back pain patients , finding it is no more effective than non-surgical interventions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neil Teitelbaum visited 25 practitioners for help with chronic pain before being treated by Thompson with trigger point injections . Teitelbaum , an electrical and computer engineer , said the treatment left him pain free for three to four years , the most effective relief he has had . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; De Beer , meanwhile , said he recommends the treatment to friends with pain problems and has been tempted to contact well-known drummers , including Phil Collins and Neil Peart of Rush , who are both known to suffer from pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Jensen , spokesman for the Ministry of Health , said the ministry supports an &amp;ldquo ; evidence-based approach&amp;rdquo ; to managing chronic pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Nerve blocks have a role in the management of patients with chronic pain but would rarely be the sole modality of treatment . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Any change to OHIP services or payment formulas , he added , would involve consultations with the Ontario Medical Association and be subject to the current binding arbitration process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;39902;TOOLONG ... of Ottawa readies test track in Nepean for autonomous vehicle technologies&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;39937;TOOLONG ... assault victim asks court to lift publication ban in her case&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;39972;TOOLONG ... a grip on icy falls in Lanark County&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Elizabeth PayneFri , 25 Jan 2019 14:21:43 **45;40007;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsMenSeniorsWomenIf Ontario scraps its LHINs , what 's next for health care ? http : **35;40054;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;40091;TOOLONG ... title= " A rare site of an empty bed in observation as the emergency department at the Queensway Carleton Hospital . " alt= " A rare site of an empty bed in observation as the emergency department at the Queensway Carleton Hospital . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;40127;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 977138 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Change is coming to health care in Ontario , and that change could mean the end of the local health hubs known as LHINs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Created by the Liberal government in 2007 , the system of 14 regional health integration networks has long been the target of critics , many of whom complain they are overly bureaucratic and wasteful , charges that local officials say are unfounded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been media reports suggesting Ontario will get rid of LHINs and could create a super agency , similar to what Alberta and British Columbia have . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the record , the province will only say that it is working on transformational change that will put patients , not bureaucracy , at the centre of the health system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our government believes that everyone in Ontario deserves to have access to the services they need at home , in the community or at a hospital . That&amp;rsquo ; s why we&amp;rsquo ; re working with partners in health care to develop our long-term transformational health-care strategy . We will continue to listen to patients , families and frontline providers , and we will create a health-care system that works for the people of Ontario , &amp;rdquo ; said Hayley Chazan , press secretary for Health Minister Christine Elliott . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But some observers are warning critics to be careful what they wish for . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Ontario is blessed with a superb cancer system , a superb organ-transplant system , a great renal network , a great cardiac network and a great critical-care network , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Bob Bell , former deputy minister of the Ministry of Health who has spoken up in support of LHINs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These are all things that aren&amp;rsquo ; t broken . As orthopedic surgeons say , if it ain&amp;rsquo ; t broke , don&amp;rsquo ; t fix it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bell said he fears quick , dramatic change to the complex health system could cause chaos that will put an end to some of the day-to day work underway to keep the system accountable and to bring about change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And that kind of work is happening . LHINs , says Bell , are good at holding hospitals and other health providers accountable , have brought in innovations . Bell cites the Champlain LHIN , which includes Ottawa , for achievements that have improved health care for patients in the region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chantale LeClerc , CEO of the Champlain LHIN , likens the work the organization does to the work of air traffic controllers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are trying to take various services and programs and knit them together in a comprehensive way . You really do need someone who has eyes on the whole and can intervene when one part of the system isn&amp;rsquo ; t doing what it needs to do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Champlain LHIN has brought in changes that have reduced wait times and expanded care at no additional cost or at a savings . Those changes include : central assessment and intake for hip and knee surgery that reduced wait times by three months at no increased cost ; community stroke rehabilitation that saves $1 million a year , reduced hospital length of stay and readmission ; and a regional orthopedic program that brings high quality surgical services closer to rural patients at no increased cost , and other innovations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Champlain LHIN board chair Jean-Pierre Boisclair says criticisms that the LHINs are bloated are unfair and untrue . &amp;ldquo ; These are not organizations that are profligate spenders . &amp;rdquo ; What is more , he said , the work being done is saving the system money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , LeClerc and Boisclair say they are ready to work with the government to implement whatever changes are brought in . Their priority , LeClerc said , is helping to ensure a smooth transition for the sake of patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Alan Drummond , an emergency physician in Perth , is among those cheering the possible demise of LHINs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The ( LHIN ) concept was valid , and in theory it should have been a good thing . I don&amp;rsquo ; t think the theory ever materialized . In a way , we have lost control over our local health-care decisions . They are being dictated to us by LHINs. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some see the LHIN system as one that was set up to fail . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Paul William , of the University of Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Institute of Health Policy , Management and Evaluation , said the LHINs were never given the tools to bring about change in the health system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I have worked extensively with the LHINs . I found them to be really thoughtful , progressive and trying really hard . But the LHINs never had the tools . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the end , Williams and other say , the system needs bigger change . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both Williams and Dr. Chris Simpson , who is vice-dean of the school of medicine at Queen&amp;rsquo ; s University , and medical director of the Southeastern Ontario Academic Medical Association , say they believe LHINs were not given what they needed to achieve real change in the system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fundamental question is whether LHINs should be abandoned , or given more authority , said Simpson . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said accountable care organizations , which focus on patient outcomes rather than the services provided , are a model the province should consider . Such integrated , nimble models follow the patients and incentivize preventative medicine and community support . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; How do you get more value for money &amp;mdash ; it is integrating . The LHINs did achieve that to some degree , but I think the current government is signaling that we need to now accelerate that process of integration . Whether they do that by restructuring the LHINs or replacing them with something else , I think that is the million-dollar question . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **27;40157;TOOLONG " **34;40186;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;40222;TOOLONG ... a killer sex attacker once deemed an eternal threat to Canadian society got parole anyway&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;40257;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Al-hadji Kudra Maliro " **28;40446;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332288 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian government has sent an emergency management expert to the Republic of the Congo to help the World Health Organization battle the worsening Ebola outbreak in nearby Democratic Republic of Congo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The outbreak is now the second largest in history after the West Africa Ebola one that killed 11,000 people between 2014 and 2016 and put the world on edge . So far , 385 people have died in DRC and the outbreak , amid ongoing conflict and contentious elections , continues to grow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Public Health Agency of Canada is &amp;ldquo ; closely monitoring the situation , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Anna Maddison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although there are new tools to fight Ebola since the 2014 crisis &amp;mdash ; notably the Canadian-developed experimental vaccine &amp;mdash ; the backdrop of political instability and violence in DRC is making it difficult to contain the outbreak . It is also causing growing alarm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ongoing and routine attacks on health workers and violence are limiting the kind of help countries are sending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;40476;TOOLONG ... title= " A member of the medical staff of the Ebola Treatment Unit ( ETU ) gets ready as she checks her Personal Protective Equipment ( PPE ) in front of the mirror . " alt= " A member of the medical staff of the Ebola Treatment Unit ( ETU ) gets ready as she checks her Personal Protective Equipment ( PPE ) in front of the mirror . " data-portal-copyright= " ISAAC KASAMANI " **28;40512;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332527 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last year , the U.S. Centres for Disease Control and Prevention pulled Ebola experts back from conflict zones in DRC over fears about their safety . There have been reports that health teams responding to the outbreak have been attacked three or four times a week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada , which is a member of WHO&amp;rsquo ; s Global Outbreak Alert and Response Network , a group that deals with threats of epidemic-prone diseases , responded to a request to send an emergency management specialist . That specialist will work at WHO&amp;rsquo ; s emergency operations centre in the neighbouring Republic of the Congo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unlike the previous West African crisis , the Public Health Agency of Canada has not , so far , sent epidemiologists , lab support or other experts directly to the outbreak region . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A PHAC spokesperson acknowledged Canada has a history of providing on-site support to WHO for Ebola . In addition to work developing the vaccine that has been deployed in DRC , the public health agency sent out epidemiologists , laboratory support and emergency management resources in 2014 and 2015 to support global efforts to respond to the West African Ebola epidemic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There have been no similar announcements this time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; When making decisions about how to support requests for assistance and subsequent deployments , &amp;rdquo ; said spokeswoman Maddison , &amp;ldquo ; PHAC considers where the greatest need exists and carefully considers the security of personnel . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;40542;TOOLONG ... title= " A health worker carries a four-day-old baby suspected of having Ebola , into a MSF ( Doctors Without Borders ) supported Ebola Treatment Centre ( ETC ) on November 4 , 2018 in Butembo , Democratic Republic of the Congo . " alt= " A health worker carries a four-day-old baby suspected of having Ebola , into a MSF ( Doctors Without Borders ) supported Ebola Treatment Centre ( ETC ) on November 4 , 2018 in Butembo , Democratic Republic of the Congo . " data-portal-copyright= " JOHN WESSELS " **28;40578;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1332289 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The decision to send an emergency management expert is one part of the Canadian response to the Ebola outbreak . The federal government has invested $1.5 million in rapid research aimed at improving the way the world responds to public health emergencies . It has also provided more than $3.3 million in emergency humanitarian assistance to WHO , the Red Cross , M&amp;eacute ; decins&amp;nbsp ; sans Fronti&amp;egrave ; res and other agencies that have health and support workers on the ground in DRC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Steven Hoffman , scientific director of the Canadian Institutes of Health Research&amp;rsquo ; s ( CIHR ) Institute of Population and Public Health , noted that responding to the current outbreak has been challenging . &amp;ldquo ; It is happening in a part of a country which is experiencing significant conflict , which makes it much more difficult for organizations and governments to help out . &amp;rdquo ; It is also happening close to a large urban area , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Hoffman said the launch of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s rapid research fund for Ebola which supports four projects with African and Canadian researchers is an important accomplishment. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; From a Canadian perspective , this is a really good news story . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;40608;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;40650;TOOLONG ... story of ' the Canadian vaccine ' that beat back **26;40685;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fund was created for the West African Ebola outbreak and was mobilized soon after the DRC outbreak began last spring , in partnership with the Social Sciences and Humanities Research Council and Canada&amp;rsquo ; s International Development Research Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Four Canadian and African research teams were chosen to work on projects during the current Ebola surge &amp;mdash ; including studying how better access to routine health information could help control outbreaks and the role social , cultural and environmental factors play in containing them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No other national government has supported similar research that can be done during the Ebola crisis and help with future ones , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The big value is that this is happening as an outbreak is occurring , which allows them to study the mechanics of the response in a way they would not be able to do when looking at it historically . &amp;rdquo ;
