I live with dementia . Let me help you understand'http : **29;40903;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;40934;TOOLONG ... title= " Keith Barrett lives with young onset dementia . He was diagnosed at 57. " alt= " Keith Barrett lives with young onset dementia . He was diagnosed at 57. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;40970;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1330085 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You don&amp;rsquo ; t look like you have dementia . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On the couch of his Orl&amp;eacute ; ans home between his wife of 15 years , Robin Meyers , and Bernese mountain dog , Brooklyn , Keith Barrett is the picture of a life well lived . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At 59 , he works full time at the company he co-founded more than 20 years ago to support people with disabilities , delights in a growing blended family of six sons and five grandchildren and is looking forward to some Florida sunshine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But he&amp;rsquo ; s also a new voice of an Alzheimer Society of Canada campaign relaunched on Monday to fight old attitudes about what the more than half a million Canadians with dementia look like . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41000;TOOLONG ... title= " Keith Barrett poses for a photo at his home in Ottawa Thursday Jan 3 , 2019 . Keith Barrett , who lives with young onset dementia diagnosed at 57 . The Ottawa couple want to reframe the conversation around this disease . " alt= " Keith Barrett poses for a photo at his home in Ottawa Thursday Jan 3 , 2019 . Keith Barrett , who lives with young onset dementia diagnosed at 57 . The Ottawa couple want to reframe the conversation around this disease . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;41036;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1330280 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; s frank that he once believed what people now thoughtlessly say to him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t want people to experience what I experienced , &amp;rdquo ; said Barrett , who was diagnosed two years ago with rare young onset dementia , Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s type , which is still in its early stages . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was part of the stigma. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The disease&amp;rsquo ; s name conjured up in his mind the image of an octogenarian , confined to a nursing home bed or a locked ward so they wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t wander . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s the picture that came to my mind and that&amp;rsquo ; s wrong , &amp;rdquo ; Barrett said . &amp;ldquo ; Through my own experiences , you can be diagnosed in your 30s , 40s , 50s . I think the more people that can share their story , the more we can have conversations and the more that our society will be accepting . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barrett , who fiercely defends his independence with cutting-edge technology , rigorous routine and the support of his wife &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; my rock&amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; chokes up as he recalls when that acceptance has been painfully missing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One person I spoke with , the first words out of their mouth : &amp;lsquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re still driving ? ' &amp;rdquo ; Barrett said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Take your time , &amp;rdquo ; said Meyers , 47 , putting a hand on his . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Keith makes really good decisions around where he drives and when he drives , &amp;rdquo ; she added . &amp;ldquo ; But I have people say to me , &amp;lsquo ; You still let him drive ? &amp;rsquo ; What do you mean , let him ? He&amp;rsquo ; s capable of making his own decisions . It&amp;rsquo ; s really a lack of understanding that people have that someone is very capable of contributing to their communities , to their lives , and to live . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41066;TOOLONG ... title= " Keith Barrett , Robin Meyers and their dog Brooklyn . Barrett lives with young onset dementia . The Ottawa couple want to reframe the conversation around this disease . " alt= " Keith Barrett , Robin Meyers and their dog Brooklyn . Barrett lives with young onset dementia . The Ottawa couple want to reframe the conversation around this disease . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;41102;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1330083 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s what we want people to understand . The moment you get diagnosed doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean you&amp;rsquo ; re the person who needs a lot of care . The moment you get diagnosed is the moment you say , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m going to work really hard to keep everything that I have , all my skills , my abilities , for as long as I can . ' &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The campaign &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; Yes . I live with dementia . Let me help you understand . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; aims to change attitudes the Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s Society uncovered with recent research that showed that one in four Canadians said they&amp;rsquo ; d feel ashamed or embarrassed if they had dementia . One in five admitted using derogatory or stigmatizing language about dementia . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That leads to real harm to patients and their families , said Alzheimer Society of Canada chief executive Pauline Tardif , who points to everything from jokes that forgetfulness must be dementia to the assumption that a diagnosis means the end of a meaningful life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It might make people afraid to seek a reason for their own or a loved one&amp;rsquo ; s symptoms like memory loss , changes in judgment and reasoning or mood and behaviour and difficulty performing familiar tasks or expressing themselves . So they don&amp;rsquo ; t get the support they need and treatment . While&amp;nbsp ; Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s disease is eventually fatal , some forms of dementia can be cured . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41132;TOOLONG ... title= " Keith Barrett lives with young onset dementia . He was diagnosed at 57. " alt= " Keith Barrett lives with young onset dementia . He was diagnosed at 57. " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;41168;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1330085 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Once diagnosed , people with dementia may face impatience when it takes them a beat longer to order a coffee or remember their PIN &amp;mdash ; Barrett was yelled at during an airport security check &amp;mdash ; leading them to shy away from tasks they can still perform and activities they enjoy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Or stigma &amp;mdash ; &amp;ldquo ; Why visit when she might not remember my kids&amp;rsquo ; names or even recognize me ? &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; can keep friends away , leaving people with dementia socially isolated , which is itself an important contributor to cognitive decline . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; People with dementia sit on the society&amp;rsquo ; s board of directors and have important roles in educating policy-makers , as Barrett did when he and Meyers attended a conference hosted by the federal health minister last spring to guide work on a new national dementia strategy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; People in our communities need to be better educated and more patient and understanding to enable them to live their full lives and not disable them prematurely , &amp;rdquo ; Tardif said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The campaign is also needed to reveal the often-invisible scale of a &amp;ldquo ; crisis&amp;rdquo ; in health care , said Tardif , who co-chairs the health minister&amp;rsquo ; s advisory board on dementia after the federal government committed to developing the strategy the society had long advocated . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While 564,000 Canadians live with some form of dementia , that number is expected to rise to 937,000 by 2031. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That&amp;rsquo ; s mostly because our society is aging and we&amp;rsquo ; re living longer . While dementia isn&amp;rsquo ; t a normal part of growing old , age is the No. 1 risk factor for developing dementia . The risk doubles every five years after age 65. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The prevalence of dementia is growing exponentially and our care partners across Canada are ill-resourced and that is causing a lot of issues in the care of people living with dementia , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; The Alzheimer Society is only one of those players . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We try to do our bests with our donors&amp;rsquo ; support but we don&amp;rsquo ; t provide health care , so in working with our provincial partners and hopefully now with the federal government with the national dementia strategy that is being created we can increase the care and support available to people living with dementia . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barrett&amp;rsquo ; s symptoms first appeared about four-and-a-half years ago . After decades of speaking effortlessly off-the-cuff for work , he started forgetting what he&amp;rsquo ; d already said . He was misplacing things and using the wrong word , say &amp;ldquo ; fridge&amp;rdquo ; instead of &amp;ldquo ; stove . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meyers recalls that Barrett would be angry that people had told him that they had had a conversation with him that he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t remember but he&amp;nbsp ; was reluctant to bring it up with doctors and they dismissed the changes as stress or aging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I knew him really well , &amp;rdquo ; Meyers said . &amp;ldquo ; I could see the changes more than anyone else could . I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t continue to watch him be as frustrated as he was . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meyers kept bringing it up . Finally , Barrett was referred to a neuropsychologist who performed cognitive tests as doctors did other blood work and an MRI to exclude other causes of memory loss . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In December of 2016 , he was diagnosed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The couple started attending Alzheimer Society education sessions and sharing the news with their family but Barrett admits that it didn&amp;rsquo ; t seem real . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I still didn&amp;rsquo ; t believe it , &amp;rdquo ; Barrett said . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m 57 at that point . It couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be right . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The message he heard was to &amp;ldquo ; go home and get your affairs in order , get your will in order . There was no positive encouragement that you can live with this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meyers , too , was left with the impression that Barrett was being advised to wind down his life and retire . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we need to do the exact opposite , &amp;rdquo ; thought Meyers , a director in a health care organization who works with seniors , many of them with dementia . &amp;ldquo ; Let&amp;rsquo ; s be positive . Let&amp;rsquo ; s live the best life we can . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two years after that diagnosis , Barrett is still heading to the office every day with the help of a colleague who supervises some of his work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He says the key to living well with dementia is constant learning : he&amp;rsquo ; s systematized his routines , starting with morning grooming and relies on technology , including an iWatch , the virtual assistant Alexa at home and GPS when driving . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41198;TOOLONG ... title= " Keith Barrett . " alt= " Keith Barrett . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell " **28;41234;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1330279 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He takes notes , asks colleagues to follow up work conversations with a short email summarizing what was discussed and Meyers , instead of telling her husband of their plans for Thursday night , for example , sends him a digital invite so it will be on his calendar . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As articulate as Keith is when he&amp;rsquo ; s talking to you , it&amp;rsquo ; s really important to understand that he&amp;rsquo ; s just affected in a way that maybe isn&amp;rsquo ; t something you can totally see every day , &amp;rdquo ; Meyers said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barrett describes losing confidence , like in his former ability to strike up a conversation with anyone , and struggling with anxiety and impatience . The noise and distraction of big social events can be overwhelming , and walks with the dog have largely replaced the movies they used to enjoy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Why pay for something you forget the next day ? &amp;rdquo ; Barrett quips , but he&amp;rsquo ; s sombre as he says that he tries not to think of his grandchildren and how he will be as they grow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; s become a passionate advocate for more services for the estimated 16,000 Canadians under 65 with young onset dementia and for research to find definitive tests for Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s disease and effective treatment in the future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;41264;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;41306;TOOLONG ... who exercise into their 50s are five times less likely to develop dementia , according to new **29;41341;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;41372;TOOLONG ... whack-a-mole has taught researchers about **29;41407;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;41438;TOOLONG ... stages of Alzheimer 's and **29;41473;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s progressively destroys brain cells . From mild impairment , it takes a path through a middle stage , when patients will eventually need help with basic activities like bathing , through a late stage when they eventually become unable to speak and need 24-hour care and finally to the end of life . How long each stage lasts is different for everyone , according to the society . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Barrett is on medication that may be able to improve his symptoms for several years but at a lower dose after difficult side effects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At least it gives you some hope , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What Meyers hears is pity when she shares that her husband lives with Alzheimer&amp;rsquo ; s and she&amp;rsquo ; s candid that it will likely take him from her too soon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On&amp;nbsp ; her wrist is a tattoo she hopes will raise awareness of dementia and start a conversation that helps spark change . It&amp;rsquo ; s Dory , the forgetful fish from Finding Nemo and her now-unforgettable motto : &amp;ldquo ; Just keep swimming . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We want people to think about us in a positive way , &amp;rdquo ; Meyers said . &amp;ldquo ; Still typically the answer is , &amp;lsquo ; Oh , I&amp;rsquo ; m very sorry . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;nbsp ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not saying that I&amp;rsquo ; m not . We don&amp;rsquo ; t want this , but it&amp;rsquo ; s here so let&amp;rsquo ; s face it in the best way that we can . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For more on the campaign , visit&amp;nbsp ; ilivewithdementia.ca. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;41504;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;41767;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 504128 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Notwithstanding the example of Prime Minister Justin Trudeau and his brother , Alexandre &amp;mdash ; each born Dec. 25 , two years apart &amp;mdash ; Christmas Day is actually the least common modern birthday across the United States and likely Canada as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Certainly that&amp;rsquo ; s the conclusion to be drawn from Matt Stiles , a data journalist who two years ago tweaked a decade&amp;rsquo ; s worth of U.S. birth statistics compiled by FiveThirtyEight , a website that specializes in statistical analysis . While Statistics Canada doesn&amp;rsquo ; t offer birthday data to the same level of detail , its monthly tabulations suggest similar patterns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stiles&amp;rsquo ; &lt;a href= " http : **34;41797;TOOLONG ... chart compiles the average number of U.S. births between 1994 and 2004 for each day of the year&lt;/a&gt; , and ranks them accordingly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The most frequent birth day is Sept. 9 , with an average 12,301 babies born . The median , Stiles reported , is around 11,000 births per day . Ranked very last in frequency is Christmas Day , when the number of births averaged fewer than 6,600. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;41833;TOOLONG ... title= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau was born on Dec. 25 , 1971. " alt= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau was born on Dec. 25 , 1971. " data-portal-copyright= " Sean Kilpatrick/The Canadian Press " **28;41869;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1322683 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The tendency of families to try and avoid giving birth on a major holiday is well known to hospitals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Mark Walker , chief of obstetrics , gynecology and newborn care at The Ottawa Hospital , said he expected there would be six to eight deliveries Christmas Day at each of the main campuses , the Civic and General . &amp;nbsp ; A quick check Tuesday with the Queensway Carleton Hospital in Ottawa revealed just one birth in the morning and none scheduled after that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We tend not to do inductions or elective C-Sections ( on the holiday ) , &amp;rdquo ; Walker said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to StatCan the most common months for giving birth across Canada are August and July , with slightly fewer than 1,100 births per day last year &amp;mdash ; compared to roughly 950 per day in December 2107. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That differs from the U.S. , where September births are the most common by a fair margin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Stiles did suggest that several of the most popular September dates &amp;ldquo ; correspond with average conception periods around Christmas . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If the most common birth dates in Canada are summertime , this would put the most popular conception periods in October and November &amp;mdash ; dovetailing with the onset of colder weather . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; How do all these numbers apply to the national capital region ? On average , fewer than 40 births occur per day during the course of the year . &amp;nbsp ; On Tuesday , if the numbers topped 20 , that would amount to a holiday surprise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;41899;TOOLONG ... ' : Trump tells boy that believing in Santa at 7 is ' marginal ' &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Staff ReporterTue , 25 Dec 2018 20:02:09 **45;42004;TOOLONG ... &amp;amp ; ChildLocal NewsSexual HealthWomenCirrhosis of the liver a ' silent epidemic ' among young adults , womenhttp : **35;42051;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;42088;TOOLONG ... title= " Stock art of a doctor with a patient . " alt= " Stock art of a doctor with a patient . " data-portal-copyright= " Tyler Olson Simplefoto " **28;42124;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1320718 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Who gets cirrhosis of the liver ? You might be surprised . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A new Ontario study has found cirrhosis rates are increasing the fastest among young adults . An epidemic of fatty liver disease is being pointed to as one possible cause for the spike . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Once considered a disease of older men , the face of cirrhosis of the liver is changing , say the authors of the study published in the journal The Lancet Gastroenterology and Hepatology on Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New cases of cirrhosis nearly doubled in Ontario between 1997 , when 6,318 people were diagnosed , and 2016 , when 12,047 people were diagnosed . Nearly one per cent of the population now has cirrhosis , according to the retrospective population-based study from the Institute for Clinical Evaluative Sciences . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;42154;TOOLONG ... title= " " alt= " " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;42190;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Young adults and women are a high-growth population for the potentially deadly liver disease . The risk of cirrhosis is 116 per cent higher for millennials who were born in 1990 than Baby Boomers born in 1951 . For women , the risk is even higher . A woman born in 1990 was 160 per cent more likely to be diagnosed with cirrhosis than a woman born in 1951. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Traditionally cirrhosis has been thought to be a disease of older men , but our data show that the face of cirrhosis is changing , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Jennifer Flemming , lead author of the study . Flemming is an adjunct scientist at ICES Queen&amp;rsquo ; s and an assistant professor at Queen&amp;rsquo ; s University . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cirrhosis of the liver is characterized by the replacement of normal liver tissue with scar tissue . Symptoms of cirrhosis include jaundice , swelling of the ankles and abdomen , itching , fatigue and loss of appetite . In general , people with fatty liver disease have no symptoms , although some may report feelings of fatigue and a general feeling of being unwell . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; More young people are being diagnosed with cirrhosis at an earlier age than they had previously , &amp;rdquo ; said Flemming. &amp;ldquo ; If these sorts of trends continue , they are going to result in a significant burden on the health-care system . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;42220;TOOLONG ... title= " An empty hospital bed . " alt= " An empty hospital bed . " data-portal-copyright= " Igor Aronov " **28;42256;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1284476 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The next step , said Flemming , is to pinpoint the exact causes for the increase in cirrhosis among young adults and women . Non-alcoholic fatty liver disease , now the leading cause of liver disease , is a likely link . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Historically , alcohol use and hepatitis C have been the most common causes of cirrhosis of the liver . But with effective treatment for hepatitis C , attention is turning to non-alcoholic fatty liver disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is present in 20-30 per cent of the population . Twenty per cent of those cases are more severe and at risk of cirrhosis , according to the study . Among people with diabetes , rates of NAFLD , as it is known , are 80 per cent . In its most severe form , the disease may result in patients needing liver transplants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although linked with obesity , it is not just a disease of people who are obese , said Flemming . Genetics put some people at higher risk . It can be reversed if a patient follows a healthy lifestyle , including a balanced diet and exercise , according to the Canadian Liver Foundation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;42286;TOOLONG ... title= " A woman lifts weight during crossfit training . " alt= " A woman lifts weight during crossfit training . " data-portal-copyright= " BERTRAND GUAY " **28;42322;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1299363 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is possible high rates of NAFLD , especially among millennials , are part of the reason for increasing cases of cirrhosis among young adults and women , says the report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alcohol also remains a key cause of cirrhosis , said Flemming . The study&amp;rsquo ; s findings should put a spotlight on drinking trends among young adults . Some studies have found that young women in North America now drink at the same rates as young men , and women are more susceptible to developing alcohol-related liver disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;42352;TOOLONG ... title= " Young couple enjoying conversation and a glass of wine . " alt= " Young couple enjoying conversation and a glass of wine . " data-portal-copyright= " kieferpix " **28;42388;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1155615 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; As with non-alcoholic fatty liver disease , certain people are at higher risk of developing cirrhosis than others , said Flemming , who works as a gastroenterologist at Kingston General Hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I see lots of people who consume amounts of alcohol that would not classify them as having an alcohol use disorder , but because of other factors it predisposes them to getting cirrhosis , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Public health strategies are needed to raise awareness about cirrhosis among young adults and women , groups not traditionally associated with a high risk of the disease . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is a public health issue as many of the causes of cirrhosis including viral hepatitis and alcohol consumption are treatable and cirrhosis can ultimately be prevented , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Flemming said it is not unusual to see young adults with cirrhosis in her clinical practice . &amp;ldquo ; We have certainly seen many young people requiring liver transplantation . The youngest person this year that I saw was 25. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And patients often have no idea before they are diagnosed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Flemming said she would like to raise awareness about the issue and also see some of the stigma removed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Strategies to increase awareness of this silent epidemic in young adults and women are needed . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; epayne@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; **29;42418;TOOLONG disease by the **33;42449;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; 7 million Canadians have non-alcoholic fatty liver disease &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 20 per cent of people with non-alcoholic steatohepatitis ( NASH ) , the most severe form of non-alcoholic fatty liver disease , can develop cirrhosis &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 11 per cent of NASH patients may die from the disease &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2016 : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 500 liver transplants took place in Canada &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 400 people were waiting for a liver transplant &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 90 children and adults died while waiting &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since 1970 , liver cancer has doubled in women &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Source : The Canadian Liver Foundation&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;42484;TOOLONG ... what happened when six colour-blind people saw colour for the first time&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;42519;TOOLONG ...
